# Fact base — v4.c04 कृत्-तद्धित-समासाः · Derivation & Compounds

Volume 4, Chapter 4: five lessons — the participles as kṛt adjectives (l01); the absolutive, the
infinitive and the agent in -तृ (l02); the secondary suffixes, taddhitas (l03); the compound as a
derivation (l04); the Chapter 4 exam (l05). Written from the Volume 4 plan
(`research/notes/vol4-plan.md`: §0–§3, §4.0–§4.0.6, the c03 block for what Chapter 3 leaves
owned, the whole c04 block §4.4, the c05.l01 block of §4.5 for the Station 0 hand-over, §5.3
(N3a, N3b), §5.9, §6, §7.1–§7.6 with the rows entered at the v4.c03 release on 2026-11-18, and
the rules settled at the Chapter 3 review printed after §7.6's table) and from sources opened
locally in this session:

- **The Aṣṭādhyāyī data file** (`corp/ashd/sutraani/data.txt`), by script: the `s`, `pc`, `an` and
  `ad` fields of every sūtra this fact base prints (174 numbers, every one found in the file; the list is §6 item 1), the
  padaccheda case labels and every bracket line (table 0.H) generated from them.
- **The Kāśikā, GRETIL e-text** (`jvkasipu.htm`), by line: every commentary line of table 0.E,
  copied character by character, each slip of the e-text corrected only where it is listed.
- **The Mahābhāṣya, GRETIL e-text** (`pmbhassu.htm`, Kielhorn's edition as the e-text tags it):
  the Paspaśāhnika lines on इन्द्रशत्रु (Pas_4.2, lines 170–171) and on स्थूलपृषती (Pas_3, lines
  157–160).
- **The Hitopadeśa, GRETIL e-text** (`hitop_u.htm`): Hit 1, kathā 1, the prose after 1.5 (line 315,
  N3a) and after 1.16 (line 365, N3b); Hit 1.14 (lines 353–354); Hit 0.31 (lines 215–216); Hit 2,
  kathā 8, the prose after 2.123 (line 2050, the retake's D′); and the DCS CoNLL-U text of the same
  N3a sentence (`Hitopadeśa-0001`), which reads *pānthāḥ* where the GRETIL e-text has *panthāḥ*.
- **The Pañcatantra, GRETIL e-text** (`vispancu.htm`): the frame story of Book 4 that the serial
  follows (lines 5261–5285; c05.l01's N4 is lines 5281–5283 and the reveal, line 5285, is not used), and Panc
  1.366 (line 2030) for उपकृत्य.
- **Whitney, *Sanskrit Grammar*** (the project's markdown of the 2nd edition): §§584, 444, 447,
  449, 452, 943, 952–960, 968, 989–993, 1098, 1182, 1202–1204, 1208, 1215, 1221–1222, 1225,
  1230, 1233, 1235, 1237, 1239, 1242, 1246–1249, 1263, 1267, 1269, 1280, 1293, 1295.
- **Apte (1890) and Monier-Williams (1899)**, the CDSL digitisations (`corp/csl`, `dict/mw`): the 26

- **The DCS** (`hitlem.txt` and the CoNLL-U files): the Hitopadeśa lemma counts of the 26
  headwords (fact base only; no count prints in learner text, row 0.13).
- **The entry ledger** (`ledger-entry-v4c04.tsv`, 1004 rows, generated by `work/owned.py` for the

  sentence, conversation, serial episode and exercise checked against it (or a headword of its

**Labels used below.** ✓ productive · ◐ recognition · ○ display. Tiers: **Q** quoted (padaccheda,
case labels), **B** bracket line and text, **D** number, text and one English line, **K** on a card,
never assessed, **TN** Teacher Notes only (no tool, no count). "(owned)" = a tool of the toolbox of
89 or a rule owned since Volume 3. Status codes of passages (plan §5): T taught, G glossed, L a

## 0. Chapter-wide facts and policies

| # | Claim | Register | Source | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.1 | **Status and tiers.** ✓ productive, ◐ recognition, ○ display, one wording everywhere. Q, B, D, K; no R in this chapter. A K rule is looked up and never assessed: no objective, exercise, quiz item, Station 0 key or exam task names it, asks it or scores a step it makes. A D-tier sūtra is never asked by number in a closed-book item; it may be named by its gist. No item demands, scores or offers a ◐ form, and no option is a non-form. | policy | STYLE_GUIDE §8; plan §1.1 principle 9, §1.4.1; plan §7.6 (the rules settled at the Chapter 2 and Chapter 3 reviews) | Applied in every lesson block below; checked in §6 item 6. |
| 0.2 | **Forms against rules (the FACT/TRADITION line).** A form printed ✓ in a lesson's table is a fact of the language, each checked against Whitney, Apte or Monier-Williams, and may be produced and scored as a form whatever rule makes it in Pāṇini's derivation — Teacher-Notes and K rules included (Chapter 3 scored द्रक्ष्यति and लप्स्यते, whose shapes rest on Teacher-Notes rules). What is never scored is the rule: no derivation task, rule question or "why" item requires or credits a step whose rule is K. Concretely: the -मान of the ✓ participles (K 7.2.82; FACT, Whitney §584b), लब्ध (K 8.2.40, 8.4.53; Apte), the active use of गत (K 3.4.72; FACT, Whitney §952a) and the -तर/-तम card (K 5.3.57, 5.3.55; FACT, Whitney §1242) are produced as forms and uses; their rules are read off the card. | policy | plan §7.6, the Chapter 3 rule "a K rule is never assessed, not even through an exercise's scoring of its result"; the c03 key S0-el, where the K-made stems of जायते and जानाति are read off the card and not scored | **Open item 1** asks the editor to confirm this reading; the alternative (no K-made shape scored anywhere) would remove -मान, लब्ध and the active गत frame from every scored item and needs a ruling, since the plan makes them ✓. |
| 0.3 | **Allowed text.** Owned words (the entry ledger, 1004 rows, 565 readable words); the chapter's headwords from their lesson on; the forms of owned words that a lesson's tables print (participles, absolutives, infinitives, agent nouns, taddhitas, compounds of owned members), from that lesson on; glossed words, each glossed where it stands. No other word prints in learner Sanskrit. | policy | STYLE_GUIDE §8 rules 16–23; plan §1.4, §3 | Kṛt forms of owned roots, transparent taddhitas and compounds of owned members add 0 to the ledger (rules 18, 19, 10); a lexicalised one counts once as a headword (वृद्धः and युद्धम्, rule 18; दैवम्, rule 19; स्वभावः, rule 10; आकर्णयति, a denominative, rule 17). |
| 0.4 | **Printing.** Every course-written line joined, its pause form beneath; a sūtra printed as the data file gives it, its tier after it in square brackets; a parenthesis directly after a Devanagari word holds that word's IAST and nothing else (every other note in square brackets); a ण्-final pratyāhāra name never directly before a vowel. Derivations: a four-column table, Step · String · Sūtra · What happens, headed "Derive, according to Pāṇini, …", at most ten lines, the last line the joined word in bold; a step whose rule is a later lesson's is one marked line; no derivation ends on a non-word (a stem is carried to its nominative, or the last line is labelled "last" with the later lesson named). Rules of 8.2–8.4 stand last in every table. | policy | STYLE_GUIDE §3.1; plan §7.6 (four-column derivations; "last"; the tripādī last) | Table 0.F holds every derivation in this form. |
| 0.5 | **Seams.** Only the owned seams print in joined learner text: m → ṃ before a consonant; m + vowel written together; a stop voiced before a voiced sound; त् + च/छ → च्च/च्छ, त् + ज → ज्ज; न् after a short vowel before a vowel → न्न (none after a long vowel); no change, written together, before a voiceless sound and for न् before other consonants; the visarga map (र + र ◐). The ◐ seams — a stop before a nasal (त् + न → न्न), न् + ज, त् + ल, त् + श → च्छ, त् + ह → द्ध, न् + च/ट/त → ंश्च/ंष्ट/ंस्त — do not occur in course-written text; an authentic passage prints them as its e-text has them. | policy | v3.c04 0.C (the sandhi maps); STYLE_GUIDE §3.1 | Two plan model sentences crossed a ◐ seam and are re-worded (§7, deviations 4 and 5). |
| 0.6 | **Registers.** What the language does is FACT, with Whitney, Apte, Monier-Williams or the DCS; what Pāṇini's rules say, and every derivation, is TRADITION, attributed ("According to Pāṇini …", "in Pāṇini's derivation") and asked only in that frame; dates are HISTORY, keyed and hedged; no SYMBOL claim. | policy | STYLE_GUIDE §4; plan §1.1 | The accent card of l04 sets TRADITION (6.1.223, 6.2.1; the Mahābhāṣya) beside FACT (Whitney §1267, §1295). |
| 0.7 | **Commentary source line.** Every Kāśikā line: "text of the GRETIL e-text" [REF:kashika]; the Mahābhāṣya: "text of the GRETIL e-text (Kielhorn's edition)" [REF:mahabhashya Paspaśāhnika]. In learner text only K-lines marked "learner" in table 0.E print; the rest stand in Teacher Notes. No printed edition was opened. | policy | plan §4.6; verification log, v4.c03 evidence rule | Every correction of an e-text slip is listed beside its line (table 0.E) and goes to the plan's §4.6 spelling list (§8 (c)). |
| 0.8 | **kṛt (3.1.93).** In the section headed by 3.1.91 धातोः, an affix after a root that is not a tiṅ ending is called kṛt. The participles (क्त, क्तवतु, शतृ, शानच्), the absolutive (क्त्वा, ल्यप्), the infinitive (तुमुन्) and the agent (तृच्) are kṛt affixes; a word ending in one is a nominal base (1.2.46, l04). | TRADITION | [REF:kashika 3.1.93] (K1, line 15604); [REF:kashika 1.2.46] (K49) | The Kāśikā's examples under 3.1.93 are gerundives (*kartavyam, karaṇīyam*: c05.l01) and are not printed in l01. |
| 0.9 | **The k-marker's three jobs** (one card, l01 §15 (3), l02 §15 (1)): before a k-marked affix (1) guṇa and vṛddhi do not apply (1.1.5, owned Q): कृत, कृत्वा, श्रुत, against कर्तुम्, श्रोतुम्, whose तुमुन् has no क्; (2) the nasal of गम्, मन्, हन्, यम्, रम्, नम् (the roots taught unaccented) and of तन् and the rest drops before a jhal (6.4.37): गत, गत्वा, मत, हत, against गन्तुम्; (3) वच्, स्वप्, यज् and the rest take samprasāraṇa (6.1.15): उक्त, उक्त्वा, सुप्त, against वक्तुम्. | TRADITION; FACT for the forms | 1.1.5 (owned, c03.l03); [REF:kashika 6.4.37] (K5, lines 63932, 63941); [REF:kashika 6.1.15] (K7, lines 51504–51525); [REF:whitney1889 §954]; §968; §991 | Whitney §954 states the same forms as facts: "the weakest form", *uktá, suptá*; "A final m is lost after a in gatá, natá, yatá, ratá". The card prints both, TRADITION first. |
| 0.10 | **Participles taught (l01).** -त (क्त) and -तवत् (क्तवतु), the niṣṭhā, for past time (3.2.102, 1.1.26); -अत् (शतृ) and -मान/-आन (शानच्) in place of laṭ (3.2.124). Production ✓: the -त in every cell, by the a-stem pattern (m. as राम, f. -ता as कमला, n. as वन); the -तवत् nominatives — m. sg. -वान्, f. sg. -वती, n. sg. -वत्, m. pl. -वन्तः; the -अत् participle's m. nom. sg. and pl. and its loc. sg. (गच्छन्, गच्छन्तः, गच्छति); every cell of -मान (a-stem). Each only for the verbs whose forms the lesson's two tables print. The rest of -अत् and -वत् declension is c05.l03's (◐); the future and perfect participles are on the inventory card (◐). | FACT (forms); TRADITION (rules) | [REF:whitney1889 §952]; §959; §960; §584; §447; §452; [REF:kashika 3.2.102] (K2); [REF:kashika 1.1.26] (K3); [REF:kashika 3.2.124] (K4) | Whitney §959: "तत् कृतवान् tát kṛtávān having done that … feminine ends in वती"; §960: the -tavant form "chiefly used predicatively … value of a personal verb-form in a past tense". |
| 0.11 | **The two frames of -त** (l01 Step 4): with a verb that takes an object, the -त form agrees with the thing done and the doer stands in the instrumental — मया पुस्तकं पठितम् (Volume 3's R2); with a verb of motion or a verb with no object, the -त form agrees with the doer — रामो वनं गतः. -तवत् always agrees with the doer: स कार्यं कृतवान्. | FACT (Whitney §952: "qualifies anything as having endured the action … When made from an intransitive or neuter verb … only an indefinite past sense: गत gone"); TRADITION (3.4.72, K: the Kāśikā's *gato devadatto grāmam*) | [REF:whitney1889 §952]; [REF:kashika 3.4.72] (K9, lines 25002, 25004) | The frame is scored as FACT (row 0.2); 3.4.72 is read off the card. v3.c02.l05's "You read R2; you do not build it (v4.c04)" is redeemed here. |
| 0.12 | **The absolutive, infinitive and agent (l02).** -त्वा (क्त्वा) for the earlier of two actions with one doer (3.4.21, Q); -य (ल्यप्) in its place in a compound whose first member is not नञ्, as after a prefix or a gati word (7.1.37, with 2.2.18 of l04 making prefix + root a compound; l04's ऊरीकृत्य, स्वीकृत्य); तुक् after a short vowel (6.1.71); the optional nasal loss before ल्यप् (6.4.38). -तुम् (तुमुन्) after a root that names the purpose of another action (3.3.10): स्नातुं गच्छति, the going is for the bathing; the -तुम् with इच्छति and शक्नोति (same doer) rests on 3.3.158 and 3.4.65 (Teacher Notes). Both are indeclinable (1.1.40; 1.1.39). The agent in -तृ: its stem is the -तुम् stem (Whitney §943); its nominative singular -ता; its cells those of दाता in T5 (v3.c02.l04: the singular and the nominative and accusative plural ✓). | TRADITION; FACT | [REF:kashika 3.4.21] (K14); [REF:kashika 7.1.37] (K15); [REF:kashika 6.1.71] (K16); [REF:kashika 3.3.10] (K17); [REF:kashika 6.4.38] (K18); [REF:kashika 1.1.40] (K19); [REF:kashika 1.1.39] (K20); [REF:whitney1889 §989]; §990; §991; §992; §968; §943; §1182 | The rule of -तृ, 3.1.133 ण्वुल्तृचौ, stays unseen for c06.l04 (plan §4.0.3); l02 names no rule for it. |

| 0.14 | **Taddhitas (l03).** The heading 4.1.76 तद्धिताः runs to the end of Book 5; a taddhita is added after a nominal base in a sense the rule names (तस्य भावः, तदस्यास्त्यस्मिन्, तत्र विदितः, तस्यापत्यम्). Before a ñ- or ṇ-marked taddhita the first vowel of the base takes vṛddhi (7.2.117, Q). Before a vowel- or य-initial taddhita the base is bha (1.4.18, owned Q) and a final इ/ई or अ/आ drops (6.4.148); before a consonant-initial one (-ता, -त्व, -मत्/-वत्) it is pada and nothing drops: एकता, बलवान्. | TRADITION; FACT (Whitney §1203a: "Before a suffix beginning with a vowel or with y … final a- and i-vowels are regularly lost"; §1204) | [REF:kashika 4.1.76] (K24); [REF:kashika 7.2.117] (K23); [REF:kashika 6.4.148] (K29); [REF:whitney1889 §1203]; §1204 | plan §7.5 P#8 (6.4.148 only where the base is bha). |
| 0.15 | **The compound as a derivation (l04).** From 2.1.3 up to the rule before 2.2.38 कडाराः कर्मधारये the rules are about samāsa (the Kāśikā: *kaḍāra-saṃśabdanāt prāg*, K45); 2.1.4 सह सुपा: a pada with a sup compounds with another; the compound is a prātipadika (1.2.46); the endings inside it are deleted (2.4.71); a first member that is a pada loses its final न् (8.2.7, owned): राज्ञः पुरुषः → राजपुरुषः. Then a samāsānta may follow (टच् after -राजन्, -अहन्, -सखि at a tatpuruṣa's end, 5.4.91: कपोतराजः, महाराजः, राजसखः), and महत् becomes महा- before a member in the same case (6.3.46). The upapada kind (2.2.19) and the ku-gati-prādi kind (2.2.18) are compounds by their own rules. | TRADITION | [REF:kashika 2.1.3] (K45); [REF:kashika 2.1.4] (K46); [REF:kashika 2.4.71] (K47); [REF:kashika 1.2.46] (K49); [REF:kashika 5.4.91] (K48); [REF:kashika 6.3.46] (K55); [REF:kashika 2.2.18] (K50); [REF:kashika 2.2.19] (K51) | Volume 3's four kinds (v3.c05.l04) are the kinds of these derivations. |
| 0.16 | **Compound accent (l04, the accent card).** According to Pāṇini a compound is accented on its last syllable (6.1.223, K) and a bahuvrīhi keeps its first member's own accent (6.2.1, K); the Mahābhāṣya gives इन्द्रशत्रुः as the word whose wrong accent turned its sense, and स्थूलपृषती as a word whose kind only the accent decides. Whitney states the same as facts of the Vedic language: tatpuruṣas whose last member is an ordinary noun "are regularly and usually accented on the final syllable" (§1267); the possessive "oftenest … has as a compound the natural accent of its prior member" (§1295). Only the meaning contrast is asked (इन्द्रस्य शत्रुः against इन्द्रः शत्रुः यस्य सः); no accent mark prints (accent: Volume 5). | TRADITION; FACT | [REF:kashika 6.1.223] (K57); [REF:kashika 6.2.1] (K58); [REF:mahabhashya Paspaśāhnika] (M1, M2); [REF:whitney1889 §1267]; §1295 | IR-026 corrected (X1). The Tvaṣṭṛ story behind इन्द्रशत्रुः is not printed (plan Q5). |

That unit's दाशरथिः under अण्, its वैयाकरणः under ठक्/ठञ्, its मतुप्/वतुप् glossed alike as "having" (p. 79) and its one -वत् for "having, like" (p. 54) are quoted from the page images and corrected in l03's Teacher Notes (4.1.95; 4.2.59; 5.2.94 against 5.2.39; 5.2.94 against 5.1.115). | FACT (what the page prints); TRADITION (the correction) | `Sanskritam-Curriculum.pdf` pp. 54, 79–80, 82, 96, 104–108, page images; plan §4.0.1 (IR rows) | The unsourced "70% of any text" (p. 106) and "3,500+ years" (p. 82) are not repeated; Teacher Notes say why (no number with a method and a source). |

### 0.A The kṛt card — the forms the lessons print (l01 §15 (2), l02 §15 (2))

Every form below is checked: the -त against Apte's root entry (its "p. p.") or Whitney §954–956;
the -त्वा and -य against Whitney §§990–992, the Kāśikā or the e-texts named; the -तुम् against Apte
(the infinitive in each root entry) or Monier-Williams; the -तृ against Monier-Williams (the stem in
-tṛ). "—" = not printed (no form checked, or not used). l01 prints columns 1–4; l02 adds 5–8. On the printed cards every note that stands here in a parenthesis after a
Devanagari word — the root after each verb, the notes of the header, every source or status note (करोति [कृ]; -त [क्त];
उपकृत्य [Panc 1.366]; कर्ता [owned]) — prints in square brackets, and so on the cards of tables 0.B–0.D: a
parenthesis after a Devanagari word holds its IAST only (row 0.4; STYLE_GUIDE §3.1).

Sources by column. **-त:** Apte's root entries, "p. p." (कृत, दत्त, उक्त, श्रुत, स्थित, गृहीत, मत,
गत, दृष्ट, ज्ञात, नीत, लब्ध, विष्ट → प्रविष्ट, जित, चरित, युक्त, पतित, कथित, खादित, लिखित, हसित,
त्यक्त, रुचित, वृद्ध, पठित); Whitney §954b–d (*uktá, suptá; sthitá; gatá*), §955f (*dattá*), §956
(*paṭhita*); the Kāśikā under 6.1.15 (*vada - uditaḥ*, line 51525). **-तवत्:** Whitney §959 ("From the past
passive participle, of whatever formation, is made, by adding the possessive suffix वन्त् vant, a
secondary derivative"): one for every -त of the card. **Present participle:** Whitney §584 (-ant/-at; -māna after an a-stem; -āna
otherwise), §447 (the cells of भवन्), §444 (the class-3 participle without n); the 3rd plural
present each verb owns (laṭ ✓ since Volume 3 or c03.l03). **-त्वा, -य:** Whitney §991 ("dā only
dattvā"; -tvā like the -ta participle), §992 (*gátya*/*gamya*; जित्य, कृत्य); the Kāśikā under 6.4.38
(*āgatya, āgamya*, line 63991), 7.1.37 (*kṛtvā*, line 67717), 3.4.21 (*snātvā*, line 24085, with
the listed correction); the Hitopadeśa e-text (*praviśya, ālocya, dṛṣṭvā, uktvā, upagamya, dattvā,
gṛhītvā*); the Pañcatantra e-text (*pradāya*; *upakṛtya*, Panc 1.366, line 2030; *ākarṇya*);
Monier-Williams s.v. ā-karṇaya ("ind.p. ā-karṇya"). **-तुम्:** Apte's root entries (*kartuṃ, gaṃtuṃ,
vaktuṃ, draṣṭuṃ, śrotuṃ, dātuṃ, jetuṃ, netuṃ, hartuṃ, bhavituṃ, carituṃ, jñātuṃ*); Whitney §968
(*kártum, cáritum, bhávitum*); the Kāśikā under 8.2.30 (*paktum*, line 78709); Monier-Williams s.v.
viś ("inf. veṣṭum") for प्रवेष्टुम्; N3b *snātuṃ*. **-तृ:** Monier-Williams s.v. kartṛ, gantṛ, vaktṛ, draṣṭṛ, śrotṛ, dātṛ, pra-veṣṭṛ; Whitney
§943 ("दातृ from √दा; कर्तृ from √कृ … han and gam show the same difference"). Not printed for want of
a checked form: स्नाता (Monier-Williams has only *a-snātṛ*), आलोचयितुम् (the plan's; no form found in the
opened texts or dictionaries); the -त of पचति (Apte gives *pakva*, the -व type of 8.2.52, a
Teacher-Notes rule) is left out on purpose. The -त of भू, पा, प्रच्छ्, हृ, इष्, जन् and their -तुम्:
Apte's root entries (*bhūta, bhavituṃ; pīta; pṛṣṭa, praṣṭuṃ; hṛta, hartuṃ; iṣṭa; jāta*).

**Status of the card (the ledger lines of l01 and l02 say so in these words).** ✓: the -त form
in every cell and the -तवत् nominatives (row 0.10) of every verb in columns 2–3; the present
participle's m. nom. sg. and pl. and loc. sg. (-अत्) and every cell of -मान/-आन, of every verb in
column 4 except ददत् (◐); the -त्वा, -य, -तुम् and -तृ forms of columns 5–8 (the -तृ in T5's ✓
cells). ◐: ददत्; every other cell of -अत् and -वत् (c05.l03); the future participle करिष्यन्

The 1.x card (p. 105, X4) gives a "future participle" as "शप् + य/यान … present + य": शप् is the
class-1 sign (c03.l02), and the future participle is the future stem with -अत् or -मान — करिष्यन्,
करिष्यमाणः, the Kāśikā's own examples under 3.3.14. The perfect participle's affix name prints in
Teacher Notes only (its rule is not opened in this chapter). The page's -य "about to [verb]" is nearest the
gerundive in -य, which Whitney calls a future passive participle (§961–962): its row is ○, c05.l01.

### 0.D The compound steps and the kinds (l04 §15 (1)–(2)); the accent card (l04 §15 (3))

Steps of every compound (TRADITION): (1) two padas with their endings, the first qualifying the
second (2.1.4, under the heading 2.1.3); (2) the whole is a prātipadika (1.2.46); (3) the endings
inside are deleted (2.4.71); (4) a first member that is a pada loses its final न् (8.2.7, owned);
(5) a samāsānta, where a rule gives one (5.4.91); (6) the new word takes its own ending.

**M1 as printed in learner text (l04).** As the e-text prints it, every seam open (STYLE_GUIDE §3.1): दुष्टः
शब्दः स्वरतः वर्णतः वा मिथ्या प्रयुक्तः न तम् अर्थम् आह। सः वाग्वज्रः यजमानम् हिनस्ति यथा इन्द्रशत्रुः स्वरतः अपराधात्॥ — pause: दुष्टः शब्दः स्वरतः
वर्णतः वा मिथ्या प्रयुक्तः न तम् अर्थम् आह। सः वाक्-वज्रः यजमानम् हिनस्ति यथा इन्द्र-शत्रुः स्वरतः
अपराधात्॥ — "A faulty word, wrongly used in accent or in sound, does not say the meaning [meant].
That word-thunderbolt harms the sacrificer, as इन्द्रशत्रुः did, through a fault of accent." Glossed:
दुष्टः "faulty", स्वरतः "in accent", वर्णतः "in sound", मिथ्या "wrongly", वाग्वज्रः "word-thunderbolt",
यजमानम् "the sacrificer", हिनस्ति "harms", अपराधात् "through a fault", इन्द्रः "Indra" (a god's name, display,
rule 12; it serves every vigraha of इन्द्रशत्रुः in l04 and in S0-fh). The e-text prints *mithyā
prayuktaḥ* as two words; the course keeps them two (मिथ्या प्रयुक्तः), as it keeps every seam the e-text leaves open. It is a verse, four lines of eleven
syllables (triṣṭubh) once its seams are joined, which the Mahābhāṣya quotes among the further
purposes of grammar (Pas_4.1, line 162: *duṣṭaḥ śabdaḥ*); the e-text sets it out as two sentences. The course calls it "a verse the Mahābhāṣya quotes" (§7, deviation 9). Source line: "Mahābhāṣya, Paspaśāhnika;
text of the GRETIL e-text (Kielhorn's edition)" [REF:mahabhashya Paspaśāhnika].

### 0.F Derivations (F-a … F-v)

Each in the four-column form of row 0.4; "marked" = a step whose rule is a later lesson's, one line.
"labelled" = a step whose rule is in Teacher Notes. The keys of the derive-it-yourself items and of
the exam's task C are these tables; a closed-book key accepts a D-tier rule named by its gist.

**F-a — Derive, according to Pāṇini, कृ + क्त → कृतम्** (l01 §8 Step 2; with the teacher)

| Step | String | Sūtra | What happens |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | कृ + क्त | 3.2.102 निष्ठा [D] · 1.1.26 क्तक्तवतू निष्ठा [D] | after a root, for past time, the niṣṭhā क्त (a kṛt, 3.1.93) |
| 1 | कृ + त | 1.3.8 लशक्वतद्धिते [B] · 1.3.9 [B] | क्, a k-row sound at the beginning of an affix that is not a taddhita, is a marker, and goes |
| 2 | कृ + त — no इट् | 3.4.114 [D] · 7.2.35 [D] · labelled: कृ takes no इट् before क्त (Teacher Notes) | क्त is ārdhadhātuka and begins with a वल् sound, so 7.2.35 would add इट्; कृ is a root that takes none |
| 3 | कृ + त — no guṇa | 7.3.84 [B] · 1.1.5 क्ङिति च [Q] | guṇa of ऋ would come before an ārdhadhātuka affix; क्त is k-marked, so it does not |
| 4 | कृत + सुँ → कृत + अम् → कृतम् | marked: the -त word is a nominal base (l04: 1.2.46) · 7.1.24 [D] · 6.1.107 [D] | the neuter nominative singular, as in मया कृतम् |
| 5 | **कृतम्** | — | joined |

| 2 | जल + ङि + जन् + अ | labelled: the ड् at an affix's beginning is a marker (Teacher Notes) | |
| 3 | जल + ङि + ज् + अ | 6.4.143 टेः [D] · 1.1.64 अचोऽन्त्यादि टि [D] | before a ḍ-marked affix the ṭi — the last vowel and what follows it, अन् — drops |
| 4 | जल + ङि + ज | — | joined: ज |
| 5 | (one word) | 2.2.19 उपपदमतिङ् [D] · 1.2.46 [D] | the upapada compounds with the kṛt-final word, before any ending of its own (the Kāśikā); the compound is a prātipadika |
| 6 | जल + ज | 2.4.71 [D] | the ending inside, ङि, is deleted |
| 7 | जलज + सुँ → जलजम् | 7.1.24 [D] · 6.1.107 [D] | the neuter nominative singular |
| 8 | **जलजम्** | — | joined: "lotus" (Monier-Williams s.v. jalaja, n., = jala-ruh, "a day-lotus") |

**F-p — Derive, according to Pāṇini, कपोतानां राजा → कपोतराजः** (l04 §8 Step 4; with the teacher)

| Step | String | Sūtra | What happens |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | कपोत + आम् + राजन् + सुँ | 2.1.4 [D] · labelled: the genitive kind (Teacher Notes) | "the king of the doves" |
| 1 | कपोत + राजन् | 1.2.46 [D] · 2.4.71 [D] | a prātipadika; the endings inside are deleted |
| 2 | कपोतराजन् + टच् | 5.4.91 राजाहस्सखिभ्यष्टच् [D] | after a tatpuruṣa ending in राजन्, the samāsānta टच् |
| 3 | कपोतराजन् + अ | 1.3.3 [B] · 1.3.9 [B] · labelled: ट् a marker (Teacher Notes) | |
| 4 | कपोतराज् + अ | labelled: before a taddhita the ṭi of a bha base in न् drops (Teacher Notes) | अन् goes |
| 5 | कपोतराज + सुँ → कपोतराजः | 8.2.66 [D] · 8.3.15 [D] | |
| 6 | **कपोतराजः** | — | joined: v3.c05.l04's pointer redeemed |

**F-q — Derive, according to Pāṇini, महान् राजा → महाराजः** (l04 Ex 3 (3), the learner)

| Step | String | Sūtra | What happens |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | महत् + सुँ + राजन् + सुँ | 2.1.4 [D] · labelled: the karmadhāraya kind (Teacher Notes) | "a great king": both members in the same case |
| 1 | महत् + राजन् | 1.2.46 [D] · 2.4.71 [D] | a prātipadika; the endings inside go |
| 2 | मह + आ + राजन् → महा + राजन् | 6.3.46 आन्महतः समानाधिकरणजातीययोः [D] · 1.1.52 [D] · 6.1.101 अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः [owned] | before a member in the same case, the last sound of महत्, its त्, → आ; then अ + आ → आ |
| 3 | महा + राजन् + टच् → महा + राजन् + अ | 5.4.91 [D] · 1.3.3 [B] · 1.3.9 [B] · labelled: ट् a marker (Teacher Notes) | the samāsānta |
| 4 | महाराज् + अ | labelled: as in कपोतराजः, the ṭi of राजन् drops (Teacher Notes) | |
| 5 | महाराज + सुँ → महाराजः | 8.2.66 [D] · 8.3.15 [D] | |
| 6 | **महाराजः** | — | joined: the Kāśikā's first example under 5.4.91 |

(Steps 2 and 3 touch different members and neither feeds the other: the key accepts them in either
order.)

**F-r — Derive, according to Pāṇini, जन्मनः दिनम् → जन्मदिनम्** (l04 Ex 3 (1), the learner)

| Step | String | Sūtra | What happens |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | जन्मन् + ङस् + दिन + सुँ | 2.1.4 [D] · labelled: the genitive kind (Teacher Notes) | "the day of birth" |
| 1 | (one word) | 1.2.46 [D] | a prātipadika |
| 2 | जन्मन् + दिन | 2.4.71 [D] | the endings inside go |
| 3 | जन्मन्दिन + सुँ → जन्मन्दिनम् | 7.1.24 [D] · 6.1.107 [D] | the new word's own ending: neuter, as दिनम् |
| 4 | जन्मदिनम् | 8.2.7 [D, owned] · labelled: जन्मन् is still a pada (Teacher Notes) | the न् at the end of a nominal base that is a pada drops — a rule of 8.2, so last |
| 5 | **जन्मदिनम्** | — | joined |

**F-s — Derive, according to Pāṇini, पङ्के जायते इति → पङ्कजम्** (l04 Ex 3 (2), the learner): F-o
with पङ्क for जल at every step (पङ्क, l02's headword); last line **पङ्कजम्** "lotus"
(Monier-Williams: "‘mud-born’, a species of lotus").

**F-t — retake C′ (1): उप + गम् + क्त्वा → उपगम्य or उपगत्य**: F-f with उप for आ at every step; the
Kāśikā's 6.4.38 gives both for an m-final root; the key accepts either. Not printed worked in any
lesson; not in any remediation exercise.

**F-u — retake C′ (2): माला + छ → मालीयम्**: F-i with माला for पाणिनि — 0 माला + ङस् · 1 + छ
(4.2.114, 1.1.73: its first vowel आ is vṛddhi) · 2 marked (2.4.71) · 3 माला + ईय (7.1.2) · 4 माल् + ईय
(6.4.148: bha, the final आ drops before ई) · 5 मालीयम् (7.1.24, 6.1.107) · **मालीयम्** ("fit for a
garland", Monier-Williams s.v. mālīya 2, citing the commentary on 1.1.73; the Kāśikā's example
under 4.2.114 and 1.1.73). Not printed worked in any lesson.

**F-v — retake C′ (3): वने जायते इति → वनजः**: F-o with वन for जल, ending masculine: 7 वनज + सुँ →
वनजः (8.2.66, 8.3.15) · **वनजः** "forest-born" (Monier-Williams s.v. vana-ja, "forest-born, sylvan,
wild"). Not printed worked in any lesson.

### 0.G 1.x quotations (`Sanskritam-Curriculum.pdf`, page images)

| # | Page | The page prints (verbatim) | What the lesson does |
|---|---|---|---|
| X1 | 82 | "Classic example: इन्द्रशत्रुर्वर्धस्व (Tvaṣṭṛ to Vṛtra) / With one accent → "May Indra's slayer prosper" (Tatpuruṣa) / With different accent → "May the slayer of Indra prosper" (Bahuvrīhi) / A single accent change → opposite meaning! / Panini codifies all this in chapters 6.1, 6.2, and 8.4." | l04 §4 and the accent card: IR-026 corrected — the two glosses say the same thing; the tatpuruṣa is इन्द्रस्य शत्रुः "Indra's enemy", the bahuvrīhi इन्द्रः शत्रुः यस्य सः "he whose enemy is Indra" (as v3.c05.l04's Teacher Notes already said). The page's "after 3,500+ years" (same page) is not repeated (row 0.18). |
| X2 | 96 | "Sutra 8.2.39: झलां जश् झशि / → "झल् changes to जश् (voiced) before झश्" / → Final stops voice when followed by another voiced stop / → लभ् + त = लब्ध (b+t → b+dh) / Sutra 8.4.53: झलां जश् जशि / → Similar: voicing assimilation" | l01 §12 and Teacher Notes: IR-017's second half — in the data file 8.2.39 is झलां जशोऽन्ते (at the end of a pada) and 8.4.53 is झलां जश् झशि (before a jhaś); लब्ध takes 8.2.40 and 8.4.53, both on the card [K] |
| X3 | 96 | "Sutra 8.2.30: चोः कुः / → "चय्-letters become कय्-letters word-finally" / → वाच् + सु → वाक्सु (च→क)" | l01 Step 3: three corrections — the rule names the c-row and the k-row (चोः, कुः), not the sound-lists चय् and कय्; the Kāśikā says before a jhal **and** at the end of a pada (K6), उक्त the first case, वाक् the second; and वाच् + सु gives वाक्षु — after क्, स् → ष् by 8.3.59, owned since v4.c02.l03, which displayed वाक्षु — not वाक्सु |
| X4 | 104–105 | "PARTICIPLES (कृदन्ताः) — Verbal Adjectives / In Sanskrit, participles do enormous work — they replace whole clauses. Six major types:" · "1. क्त (KTA) — past passive participle ("-ed") / Meaning: "having been [verb]ed" (passive past)" · "5. शप् + य/यान (FUTURE PARTICIPLE) / Means "about to [verb]" — present + य." | l01 §15 (4), the inventory card: IR-024 corrected (row 0.B); and "passive" qualified — with a verb of motion or no object the -त is not passive (गतः "gone", Whitney §952a) |
| X5 | 104 | "3. शतृ … For parasmaipada verbs. Formation: present stem + अत् (with marker श्) / √पठ् → पठ् + अत् → पठन्त् → पठन् ("reading") / √गम् → गच्छ + अत् → गच्छन्त् → गच्छन्" | l01 Step 5: kept, with the sign शप् shown (पठ + अत्) and the ऋ named as the second marker |
| X6 | 106 | "Lesson 11.23 — Gerund & Infinitive / क्त्वा-तुमुन्" · "The two most COMMON non-finite forms in Sanskrit prose. Master these and you can read 70% of any text." · "Sutra: समानकर्तृकयोः पूर्वकाले (3.4.21) → "For two acts with the same agent, the earlier one takes ktvā"" | l02: the 1.x lesson restored (its forms, its 3.4.21 gloss kept); the "two most COMMON" and the "70%" not repeated (no number with a method and a source; Teacher Notes say so) |
| X7 | 106 | "Sutra: समासेऽनञ्पूर्वे क्त्वो ल्यप् (7.1.37) → "After a prefix (other than नञ्), क्त्वा becomes ल्यप् → -य"" | l02 Step 3: corrected — "in a compound whose first member is not नञ्"; नञ् is the negative अ-, not a prefix (the Kāśikā's counter-example अकृत्वा, K15); a prefix makes a compound by 2.2.18 (l04) |
| X8 | 107 | "Sutra: तुमुन्ण्वुलौ क्रियायां क्रियार्थायाम् (3.3.10) → "When the action is for the sake of another action, use tumun"" · "रामः ग्रामं गन्तुम् इच्छति।" · "सः वेदं पठितुम् आगच्छति।" · "A single Sanskrit sentence can string 5+ ktvā/lyap clauses before the final verb." | l02 Step 4: the gloss made exact — the page does not say which action takes तुमुन्; Step 4's line puts it on the root that names the purpose (पठितुम्), the action done for its sake (आगच्छति) standing beside it; सः वेदं पठितुम् आगच्छति is 3.3.10's kind; रामः ग्रामं गन्तुम् इच्छति rests on another rule (3.3.158, Teacher Notes); "5+" not repeated — the Kāśikā's own chain under 3.4.21 strings three (*snātvā pītvā bhuktvā vrajati*) |

### 0.I Station 0 — canonical wording and keys

Each booking prints verbatim in the booking lesson's §20 and in the running lesson's §3, without
the labels, as "- +1 day: …" / "- +1 week: …". The keys print in the running lesson's §3 under
`<details><summary>उत्तराणि · Station 0 key</summary>`, ✓ marks only. A closed-book key accepts a
D-tier sūtra named by its gist; no key asks or credits a K rule. Each item is timed from what it
asks (the Chapter 3 ruling: a derivation of about eight lines from memory, five minutes — four to
write, one to mark); the minutes come out of the class plan of the running lesson (45 in all).

| Label | Booked by | Run by | Wording | Minutes |

| S0-ej | v4.c03.l05 | v4.c04.l01 | "+1 week: derive करिष्यति — derive, according to Pāṇini, in table form, कृ + लृट् → करिष्यति, each step with its sūtra; v4.c04.l01's Station 0 runs exactly this." | 5 |

  विधिलिङ् भवेत् · भवेताम् · भवेयुः; लिट् बभूव · बभूवतुः · बभूवुः; लृट् भविष्यति · भविष्यतः · भविष्यन्ति
  (v4.c03.l06 §15 (1), Whitney §735, §742, §739, §738, §793, §800, §§932–933). 18 cells and 6 names.

  base); 3.3.13, 3.1.33, 3.4.114, 7.2.70, 1.1.51 and 8.3.59 may be named by their gist; 7.3.84 and 1.3.3,
  1.3.9 (B) by number or gist.
- **S0-el** (l02): as the Chapter 3 fact base keys it: तिष्ठति — 1 · शप् · तिष्ठ [7.3.78, the card
  open] · जायते — 4 · श्यन् · जाय [7.3.79, a card rule: read off the card, not scored] · पृच्छति — 6 · श ·
  पृच्छ · शृणोति — 5 (by its sign; श्रु is bhvādi, 3.1.74) · श्नु · शृणु-/शृणो- · अस्ति — 2 · none · अस्-/स्-
  · ब्रवीति — 2 · none · ब्रू-/ब्रवी- · युनक्ति — 7 · श्नम् · युनज्-/युञ्ज्- · जानाति — 9 · श्ना · जाना-/जानी-
  [7.3.79, read off the card, not scored] · चिन्तयति — 10 · णिच् + शप् · चिन्तय · स्वपिति — 2 · none · स्वप्
  (+ इट्, 7.2.76).
- **S0-fa** (l02): कृतः कृतवान् · गतः गतवान् · उक्तः उक्तवान् · पठितः पठितवान् · दृष्टः दृष्टवान् · स्थितः
  स्थितवान् · लब्धः लब्धवान् · दत्तः दत्तवान् (l01 §15 (1); row 0.2: forms, no rule asked).
- **S0-fb** (l03): F-c, eight lines; then: गम् + क्त loses its nasal instead — 6.4.37, or by its gist
  ("the nasal of गम् drops before a k-marked affix beginning with a jhal").
- **S0-fc** (l03): (1) मकरो वानरं दृष्ट्वा वदति। (2) रामो ग्रामं गत्वा मित्रं पश्यति। (3) छात्रः कथां श्रुत्वा
  हसति। (4) नृपो गृहं प्रविश्योपविशति। (5) पान्थो नद्यां स्नात्वा भोजनं खादति। (6) माता पुत्राय फलं दत्त्वा
  गृहं गच्छति। One point each: the absolutive right and the sentence joined.
- **S0-fd** (l04): F-f, either route.
- **S0-fe** (l04): एकता · गुरुत्वम् · बालत्वम् · धनवान् · श्रीमान् · बली.

  line may be written "the ending inside goes (2.4.71)".
- **S0-fg** (l05): (1) राजपुरुषः — tatpuruṣa (genitive) · (2) जन्मदिनम् — tatpuruṣa (genitive) ·
  (3) कपोतराजः — tatpuruṣa (genitive), with -राज · (4) महाराजः — karmadhāraya · (5) सिंहमूषकौ — dvandva ·
  (6) नीलकण्ठः — bahuvrīhi. One point each: the compound and its kind.

  शत्रुः यस्य सः — "he whose enemy is Indra". No accent is asked.
- **S0-fi** (c05.l01): (1) बालको विद्यालयं गत्वा मित्रं दृष्ट्वा हसति। (2) पान्थो ग्रामं प्रविश्य कथां श्रुत्वा
  गृहं गच्छति। (3) माता गृहमागत्य पुत्राय फलं दत्त्वा स्वपिति। (आगम्य accepted for आगत्य.)
- **S0-fj** (c05.l02): पतितः पतितवान् पतन् · कथितः कथितवान् कथयन् · खादितः खादितवान् खादन् · लिखितः लिखितवान्
  लिखन् · ज्ञातः ज्ञातवान् जानन् · गृहीतः गृहीतवान् गृह्णन् (l01 §15 (2)).

Seams in the bookings (row 0.5): every join is an owned one — ओ for अः before a voiced sound (मकरो
वानरम्), the visarga dropped before a vowel other than अ (नृप उपविशति), ṃ before a consonant, m + vowel
written together (गृहमागच्छति), a + u → o (प्रविश्योपविशति), सिंहश्च (visarga → श् before च, the visarga
map). The +1-week items S0-fb, S0-fd and S0-ff are derivations printed worked in their booking
lessons; the exam's task C uses none of S0-ff's and S0-fg's items (l05 §3, §18).

### 0.J Passages

**N3a — l01 (sentence 1) and l02 (sentence 2).** Hit 1, kathā 1, the prose after Hit 1.5
(`hitop_u.htm` line 315). The pigeon king tells his flock the story (the frame: lines 307–313, the question *kapotā ūcuḥ-katham etat ?* at line 312).

Sentence 1 — printed in l01:

> सोऽब्रवीत् — अहमेकदा दक्षिणारण्ये चरन्नपश्यमेको वृद्धो व्याघ्रः स्नातः कुशहस्तः सरस्तीरे ब्रूते —
> भो भो पान्थाः! इदं सुवर्णकङ्कणं गृह्यताम्।

Pause: सः अब्रवीत् — अहम् एकदा दक्षिण-अरण्ये चरन् अपश्यम् एकः वृद्धः व्याघ्रः स्नातः कुश-हस्तः
सरः-तीरे ब्रूते — भोः भोः पान्थाः! इदम् सुवर्ण-कङ्कणम् गृह्यताम्।

IAST (the e-text's, its one slip corrected): *so 'bravīt-aham ekadā dakṣiṇāraṇye carann apaśyam eko
vṛddho vyāghraḥ snātaḥ kuśa-hastaḥ saras-tīre brūte-bho bho pānthāḥ ! idaṃ suvarṇa-kaṅkaṇaṃ
gṛhyatām |*

"He said: 'Once, wandering in the southern forest, I saw an old tiger, bathed, kuśa-grass in hand,
on the shore of a lake, saying: "O travellers! Take this gold bracelet."'" (gṛhyatām is "let it be
taken"; the gloss gives both.)

Sentence 2 — printed in l02:

> ततो लोभाकृष्टेन केनचित्पान्थेन आलोचितम् — भाग्येन एतत्सम्भवति।

Pause: ततः लोभ-आकृष्टेन केनचित् पान्थेन आलोचितम् — भाग्येन एतत् सम्भवति।

IAST: *tato lobhākṛṣṭena kenacit pānthena ālocitam-bhāgyena etat sambhavati |* — "Then a traveller,
drawn by greed, thought: 'This comes about by luck.'"

**D′ — the retake of task D, unseen.** Hit 2, kathā 8, the prose after Hit 2.123 (`hitop_u.htm`
line 2050), its first two sentences:

> ततः शशकस्तं गृहीत्वा गभीरकूपं दर्शयितुं गतः। तत्रागत्य स्वयमेव पश्यतु स्वामीत्युक्त्वा तस्मिन्कूपजले
> तस्य सिंहस्यैव प्रतिबिम्बं दर्शितवान्।

Pause: ततः शशकः तम् गृहीत्वा गभीर-कूपम् दर्शयितुम् गतः। तत्र आगत्य स्वयम् एव पश्यतु स्वामी इति
उक्त्वा तस्मिन् कूप-जले तस्य सिंहस्य एव प्रतिबिम्बम् दर्शितवान्।

IAST: *tataḥ śaśakas taṃ gṛhītvā gabhīra-kūpaṃ darśayituṃ gataḥ | tatrāgatya svayam eva paśyatu
svāmīty uktvā tasmin kūpa-jale tasya siṃhasyaiva pratibimbaṃ darśitavān |* — "Then the hare took
him and went to show [him] a deep well. Having come there, and having said, 'Let my lord see for
himself', he showed [him] in the water of the well the reflection of that very lion."

## 1. L1 — क्तः शतृ: Done and Doing — Participles as kṛt Adjectives (grammar)

**Front matter.** id `v4.c04.l01` · file `lesson-01-done-and-doing.md` · title sa क्तः शतृ · sa_iast
*ktaḥ śatṛ* · en "Done and Doing: Participles as kṛt Adjectives" · hi "किया हुआ और करता हुआ: कृत्
प्रत्यय से बने विशेषण" · lesson_type grammar · cefr B1 · duration 45 · self_study_minutes **50** ·
prerequisites [v4.c03.l06] · review_of [v4.c03.l06, v4.c03.l05, v4.c03.l03, v3.c02.l05, v1.c02.l03,
v1.c03.l01] · vocabulary_count **8** · tags [grammar, panini, krt, participle, kta, ktavatu, shatr,
shanac, nishtha, k-marker, hitopadesha, reading, serial, spaced-review] · media (planned): image
`v4c04l01-done-and-doing.svg` (one root, two arrows: क्त to "done", शतृ to "doing"; the क् of क्त
lit, with three small doors behind it — no guṇa, the nasal gone, व् → उ); audio
`v4c04l01-derivation-read.mp3`, `v4c04l01-n3a-read.mp3`, `v4c04l01-conversation.mp3`; worksheet
`v4c04l01-participles.pdf`; quizzes `v4c04l01-quiz.md`, `v4c04l01-quiz-key.md`.

**Objectives** (one exercise each; §18):
1. en "Form the -त and -तवत् participles of the verbs on the kṛt card, and use them in their two
   frames: मया पुस्तकं पठितम्, and रामो वनं गतः, स कार्यं कृतवान्." · hi "कृत्-कार्ड की क्रियाओं के -त
   और -तवत् रूप बनाएँ और उन्हें उनके दो ढाँचों में प्रयोग करें: मया पुस्तकं पठितम्, और रामो वनं गतः, स
   कार्यं कृतवान्।" (Ex 1, 8 items)
2. en "Form the present participle of the card's verbs in the cells this lesson prints, and use it
   for an action going on at the same time." · hi "कार्ड की क्रियाओं का वर्तमान कृदन्त, इस पाठ में छपे
   रूपों में, बनाएँ और साथ-साथ चल रही क्रिया के लिए उसका प्रयोग करें।" (Ex 2, 6 items)
3. en "Derive, according to Pāṇini, कृतम् and गतः with the teacher and उक्तम् and पठितम् by yourself,
   and say what the क् of क्त does in four more verbs." · hi "पाणिनि के अनुसार शिक्षक के साथ कृतम् और गतः
   की, और स्वयं उक्तम् और पठितम् की व्युत्पत्ति करें; और चार और क्रियाओं में बताएँ कि क्त का क् क्या करता
   है।" (Ex 3, 6 items)
4. en "Read the first sentence of the Hitopadeśa's story of the old tiger, joined, with four glosses,
   and answer six questions on it." · hi "हितोपदेश की बूढ़े बाघ वाली कथा का पहला वाक्य, सन्धि सहित और
   चार टिप्पणियों के साथ, पढ़ें और उस पर छह प्रश्नों के उत्तर दें।" (Ex 4, 6 items)

**The opening (§1).** Volume 1 gave कृत "done" (v1.c02.l03) and गत "gone" (v1.c03.l01) as whole words;
Volume 3 read मया कृतम् "done by me" and said: "You read R2; you do not build it (v4.c04)."
(v3.c02.l05 §8, quoted from the file: "You read R2; you do not build\nit (v4.c04).") Today the
learner builds it. Question: why is "gone" गत, with no trace of the म् of गम्?

**The one grammar point.** An affix after a root that is not a verb ending is a kṛt (3.1.93). Two
kṛt affixes make the participles of this lesson: the niṣṭhā क्त/क्तवतु for the past (3.2.102), and
शतृ/शानच् in place of laṭ for "while doing" (3.2.124). The क् of क्त decides the root's shape:
no guṇa (1.1.5), the nasal of गम् gone (6.4.37), व् of वच् → उ (6.1.15).

v3.c02.l05, v1.c02.l03 and v1.c03.l01 (the prompts).

### §4 Callouts

- **TRADITION.** According to Pāṇini, an affix that comes after a root and is not a verb ending is
  called kṛt (3.1.93 [D]); the participles, the absolutive, the infinitive and the agent noun of this
  chapter are all kṛt. [REF:ashtadhyayi 3.1.93]; [REF:kashika 3.1.93]
- **FACT.** Whitney: the participle in -त, "when coming from transitive verbs, qualifies anything as
  having endured the action expressed by the verb: thus, दत्त dattá given; उक्त uktá spoken … When
  made from an intransitive or neuter verb, the same participle … has no passive but only an
  indefinite past sense: thus, गत gatá gone; भूत bhūtá been; पतित patitá fallen." [REF:whitney1889 §952]
- **FACT.** The -तवत् form, in the later language, "is chiefly used predicatively, and oftenest
  without copula expressed, or with the value of a personal verb-form in a past tense"; Whitney's
  example, *māṁ na kaścid dṛṣṭavān* "no one has seen (or saw) me". [REF:whitney1889 §960]
- **HISTORY.** The Hitopadeśa, the book of fables the reading comes from, is dated c. 9th–12th
  century CE on the scholarly estimate. [REF:hitopadesha] (the wording v4.c03.l04 printed,
  search-confirmed at that chapter's review)

### §5 What the lesson explains (plain text)

The 1.x book (p. 104, X4) calls क्त the "past passive participle", "having been [verb]ed". That is
right for a verb that takes an object — मया पुस्तकं पठितम् "the book (was) read by me" — and wrong for
a verb of motion or one with no object: गतः is "gone", not "been gone" (Whitney §952a). The lesson
keeps the English name and states its condition. It corrects two more 1.x lines: the "future
participle" of p. 105 (IR-024; §15 (4)), and p. 96's "Sutra 8.2.39: झलां जश् झशि" (IR-017, second
half): in the data file 8.2.39 is झलां जशोऽन्ते and 8.4.53 is झलां जश् झशि — the rule that makes the
ब् of लब्ध; with 8.2.40, which makes its ध, it stands on the card [K] (X2).

### §6–§7 Sanskrit and Hindi explanation

§6, three lines, each joined with its pause form beneath (owned words and the course's terms only):
1. **कृदिति प्रत्ययो धातोः परः, तिङ् न।** — pause: कृत् इति प्रत्ययः धातोः परः, तिङ् न। — "kṛt: an affix
   after a root, not a tiṅ."
2. **क्तस्य ककार इत्, अतः कृत इति रूपे गुणो न भवति।** — pause: क्तस्य ककारः इत्, अतः कृतः इति रूपे गुणः
   न भवति। — "The क् of क्त is a marker; so in the form कृत there is no guṇa." (ककारः, इत्, रूपम्, गुणः:
   owned since v4.c01–c03.)
3. **गच्छन्बालकः, पठितं पुस्तकम्।** — pause: गच्छन् बालकः, पठितम् पुस्तकम्। — "a boy going; a book read."
Seams: त् → द् before a vowel (कृदिति); ओ for अः before a voiced sound; अः before a vowel other than अ
drops its visarga (ककार इत्, कृत इति); ṃ; न् before ब written together.
§7 (Hindi) explains the two frames with Hindi's "किया हुआ" / "करता हुआ" — the Hindi glosses of the
headings only; no Hindi FACT callout (row 0.17).

### §8 English explanation — the steps

**Step 1 — kṛt.** D — **3.1.93 कृदतिङ्** [D]. Bracket line (0.H). English: "In this section on roots,
an affix that is not a tiṅ is called kṛt." [REF:ashtadhyayi 3.1.93]; [REF:kashika 3.1.93]

**Step 2 — the niṣṭhā, and what its क् does.** D — **3.2.102 निष्ठा** [D] (bracket: भूते, 3.2.84, by
name) — "The niṣṭhā comes after a root for past time." D — **1.1.26 क्तक्तवतू निष्ठा** [D] — "क्त and
क्तवतु are called niṣṭhā." The Kāśikā adds what the letters are for: the क् "for the work of a
k-marker", the उ of क्तवतु "for the work of a u-marker" (K3; the latter is c05.l03's: the -वन्त् of
the strong cells). With the teacher: F-a (कृतम्) and F-b (गतः), side by side. D — **6.4.37
अनुदात्तोपदेशवनतितनोत्यादीनामनुनासिक लोपो झलि क्ङिति** [D] (bracket: नलोपः, 6.4.23 — the file carries it; this
rule does not use it) —
"Before a k- or ṅ-marked affix beginning with a jhal, the nasal of the roots taught unaccented (गम्,
मन्, हन्, यम्, रम्, नम्), of वन् and of तन् and the rest drops." (The six roots are the ones the Kāśikā
names, K5.) The k-marker card
(§15 (3)) prints the three jobs: no guṇa (कृत, श्रुत), the nasal gone (गत, मत, हत), and, Step 3, व् → उ.
No इट् in कृत and गत: both roots take none before क्त (a labelled step; the rule is in Teacher Notes),
while पठ् does — पठित, by 7.2.35 [D, owned], as in पठिष्यति.

**Step 3 — उक्त: two more tools.** D — **6.1.15 वचिस्वपियजादीनां किति** [D] — "Before a k-marked
affix, वच्, स्वप्, and यज् and the rest take samprasāraṇa." It stood on c03.l04's card [K], for ऊचुः; it is
a tool from today. D — **8.2.30 चोः कुः** [D] (bracket: झलि, 8.2.26;
अन्ते, 8.2.29) — "A c-row sound becomes a k-row sound before a jhal and at the end of a pada." Two
cases, as the Kāśikā gives them (K6): before a jhal — उक्त, वक्तुम् (l02); at a pada's end — वाक्.
The 1.x page (X3, p. 96: "चय्-letters become कय्-letters word-finally" · "वाच् + सु → वाक्सु") is wrong three
times: "word-finally" gives only the second case; the letters are the c-row and the k-row (चु, कु), not the
sound-lists चय् and कय्; and वाच् + सु is वाक्षु — after क्, स् → ष् by 8.3.59, as v4.c02.l03 showed. The learner's derivations, उक्तम् and
पठितम्, are Ex 3 (1)–(2); their keys are in §18, not here.

**Step 4 — the two frames.** With a verb that takes an object, the -त form agrees with the thing
done and the doer stands in the instrumental: **मया पुस्तकं पठितम्** — Volume 3's R2, now built. With
a verb of motion or a verb with no object, the -त form agrees with the doer: **रामो वनं गतः**,
**सीता वनं गता**. The -तवत् form always agrees with the doer: **स कार्यं कृतवान्**, **सा कार्यं
कृतवती**. Card line [K]: 3.4.72 (the -त of a verb of motion or with no object is for the doer); it
is read off the card, never asked (row 0.2). The feminine -ता: the affix of its -आ is lesson 3's
(4.1.4).

**Step 5 — while doing: शतृ and शानच्.** D — **3.2.124 लटः शतृशानचावप्रथमासमानाधिकरणे** [D] (bracket:
वर्तमाने, 3.2.123) — "laṭ is replaced by शतृ and शानच् when [the participle] agrees with a word not in
the nominative." The Kāśikā's examples: पचन्तं देवदत्तं पश्य "see Devadatta cooking"; then it adds
that sometimes it agrees with a nominative too, सन् ब्राह्मणः (K4). The stories' use — **बालको ग्रामं गच्छन्हसति**, the participle marking how the
main action goes — rests on a further rule (Teacher Notes). The ending names: शतृ's श् and ऋ are markers (1.3.8, 1.3.2 [B]), leaving
-अत्; शानच्'s -आन is ātmanepada (1.4.100 [D, owned]: "taṅ and āna are called ātmanepada"), so an Ā
verb takes it: after a stem in -अ, -मान (लभमानः; its म् by 7.2.82, a card rule [K]; Whitney §584b:
"After a tense-stem ending in a, the middle participial suffix is māna instead of āna"); after
another stem, -आन (कुर्वाणः, its ण् by 8.4.2 [D, owned]). A shortcut for the -अत् stem: take the 3rd
plural present and drop its -इ — गच्छन्ति → गच्छन्त्-: गच्छन् (m. nom. sg.), गच्छन्तः (m. nom. pl.); the
weak stem without न्, गच्छत्-, gives the locative singular गच्छति. The shortcut holds for every verb on
the card; ददति → ददत्- (Whitney §444: the class-3 participle has no न्) is listed, ◐. F-e shows गच्छन्
step by step; no task asks it (its अ + अ → अ is c06.l04's rule).

**Step 6 — the inventory (IR-024).** §15 (4): present, future, perfect and past participles, each
with its affix and status. The 1.x card's "शप् + य/यान (FUTURE PARTICIPLE) … present + य" (X4) is
corrected: शप् is the class-1 sign (c03.l02), and the future participle is the future stem with -अत्
or -मान — करिष्यन्, करिष्यमाणः, the Kāśikā's own examples (K11; 3.3.14 [K]). ◐: recognised, never made.

### §9–§11 Pronunciation, IPA, mouth

Drill (read aloud, twice): कृत /kr̩t̪ɐ/ · गत /gɐt̪ɐ/ · उक्त /ukt̪ɐ/ · पठित /pɐʈʰit̪ɐ/ · दृष्ट /d̪r̩ʂʈɐ/ ·
स्थित /st̪ʰit̪ɐ/ · लब्ध /lɐbd̪ʱɐ/ · दत्त /d̪ɐt̪t̪ɐ/ · कृतवान् /kr̩t̪ɐʋɑːn̪/ · गच्छन् /gɐt͡ɕt͡ɕʰɐn̪/ · लभमानः
/lɐbʱɐmɑːn̪ɐh/ · कुर्वाणः /kuɾʋɑːɳɐh/. Mouth: in ब्ध the lips close for ब and open on a breathy ध at the
teeth — no vowel between; in दृष्ट the tongue curls back for ष and stays curled for ट; in दत्त hold the
त, do not release it twice.

### §12 Common mistakes (six rows)

| Mistake | Fix | Why |
|---|---|---|
| \*अहं पुस्तकं पठितः | मया पुस्तकं पठितम्, or अहं पुस्तकं पठितवान् | with an object, -त agrees with the thing done; -तवत् agrees with the doer (Step 4) |
| \*सीता वनं गतः | सीता वनं गता | the -त form is an adjective: it takes the doer's gender |
| \*गम्तः | गतः | the nasal of गम् drops before क्त (6.4.37 [D]) |
| \*वक्तः | उक्तः | व् → उ before a k-marked affix (6.1.15 [D]) |
| \*करितः | कृतः | कृ takes no इट् before क्त, and the क् blocks guṇa (1.1.5 [Q]) |
| \*बालका ग्रामं गच्छन्हसन्ति | बालका ग्रामं गच्छन्तो हसन्ति | many doers: the participle in the plural, गच्छन्तः |

### §13 Examples — model sentences (owned words and this lesson's headwords only)

1. **रामो वनं गतः।** — pause: रामः वनम् गतः। — *rāmo vanaṃ gataḥ.* — "Rāma has gone to the forest."
2. **मया पुस्तकं पठितम्।** — pause: मया पुस्तकम् पठितम्। — *mayā pustakaṃ paṭhitam.* — "The book has
   been read by me."
3. **स कार्यं कृतवान्।** — pause: सः कार्यम् कृतवान्। — *sa kāryaṃ kṛtavān.* — "He did the work."
4. **बालको ग्रामं गच्छन्हसति।** — pause: बालकः ग्रामम् गच्छन् हसति। — *bālako grāmaṃ gacchan hasati.* —
   "The boy laughs as he goes to the village."
5. **वृद्धो व्याघ्रः सरस्तीरे तिष्ठन्पान्थं पश्यति।** — pause: वृद्धः व्याघ्रः सरः-तीरे तिष्ठन् पान्थम्
   पश्यति। — *vṛddho vyāghraḥ sarastīre tiṣṭhan pānthaṃ paśyati.* — "An old tiger, standing on the
   shore of a lake, sees a traveller."
6. **भाग्येन धनं लब्धं मूर्खेण।** — pause: भाग्येन धनम् लब्धम् मूर्खेण। — *bhāgyena dhanaṃ labdhaṃ
   mūrkheṇa.* — "By luck, wealth was gained by a fool."

Seams: ओ for अः before a voiced sound (1, 4, 5); ṃ (all); न् before a consonant, written together
(गच्छन्हसति, तिष्ठन्पान्थम्); the visarga of सरः before त → स् inside the compound (the visarga map).
No ◐ seam. Sentence 6 is the plan's, its order kept (the doer last, for weight).

### §14 Vocabulary

Headword table: table 0.K, rows l01 (8). Ledger line, as printed:

> Ledger 565 → 573 (8 ledger-new). Cells drilled: 18 (Ex 1: जितः, जितवान्, त्यक्तः, त्यक्तवान्, नीतः,
> नीतवान्, श्रुतः, श्रुतवान्, लिखितम्, खादितवान्, गतवती, कथितवान्; Ex 2: पठन्, गच्छन्, तिष्ठन्, पठन्तः,
> लभमानः, खादन्). Tools added: 7 (3.1.93, 3.2.102, 1.1.26, 3.2.124, 6.4.37, 8.2.30, 6.1.15); toolbox 96.
> ✓ covers only the cells this lesson's tables print and the cells owned since Volume 3: the -त
> form in every cell and the -तवत् nominatives (m. sg. -वान्, f. sg. -वती, n. sg. -वत्, m. pl. -वन्तः)
> of the verbs of §15 (1)–(2); the present participle's masculine nominative singular and plural and
> its locative singular (-अत्), and every cell of -मान and -आन, of the same verbs, ददत् excepted (◐).

Ledger arithmetic: none of the eight owned before (entry ledger); वृद्धः counts once as a
lexicalised participle (rule 18); every -त, -तवत् and present participle of an owned verb counts
0 (rule 18).

### §15 Grammar — the cards

1. **The forms table** (8 verbs; the plan's): Root · -त · -तवत् · present participle · the tool —
   कृ कृत कृतवान् कुर्वन् · कुर्वाणः [1.1.5 no guṇa; ṇ by 8.4.2] · गम् गत गतवान् गच्छन् [6.4.37] · वच्
   उक्त उक्तवान् — [6.1.15; 8.2.30] · पठ् पठित पठितवान् पठन् [7.2.35] · दृश् दृष्ट दृष्टवान् पश्यन् [Teacher
   Notes: the ष्ट] · स्था स्थित स्थितवान् तिष्ठन् [Teacher Notes: the इ] · लभ् लब्ध लब्धवान् लभमानः [the ब्ध:
   8.2.40, 8.4.53, card rules K] · दा दत्त दत्तवान् ददत् ◐ [Teacher Notes: दद्].
2. **The kṛt card**, columns 1–4 of table 0.A (the -त, -तवत् and present participle of every verb
   there), with its sources in Teacher Notes.
3. **The k-marker card** (row 0.9): three jobs, with the l01 examples (कृत, श्रुत · गत, मत, हत · उक्त,
   सुप्त); l02 adds the -त्वा and -तुम् columns.
4. **The inventory card** (table 0.B): seven rows, present ✓, past ✓, future and perfect ◐, the gerundive ○ (c05.l01).
5. **The two frames** (Step 4), on one card: with an object — the thing done agrees, the doer in the
   instrumental (मया पुस्तकं पठितम्); motion or no object — the doer agrees (रामो वनं गतः); -तवत् — the
   doer agrees (स कार्यं कृतवान्).
6. **Paradigm ledger at exit of l01:**

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| -त, every cell (a-stem pattern), of the verbs of (1)–(2) | ✓ |
| -तवत् nominatives (m. sg., f. sg., n. sg., m. pl.) | ✓ |
| -अत्: m. nom. sg. and pl., loc. sg. — of the verbs of (1)–(2), ददत् excepted | ✓ |
| -मान / -आन: every cell | ✓ |
| the other cells of -अत् and -वत् (c05.l03); ददत्; the feminine -अन्ती | ◐ |
| future and perfect participles (करिष्यन्, करिष्यमाणः; -वांस्) | ◐ |
| the two frames of -त; the -तवत् frame | ✓ |

### §16 Conversation (eleven turns; homework, §21)

Two learners, गोपालः and सीता, after the weekend. Each turn: joined; pause form; IAST; English.

1. सीता — **गोपाल, ह्यस्त्वं किं कृतवान्?** — pause: गोपाल, ह्यः त्वम् किम् कृतवान्? — *gopāla, hyas tvaṃ
   kiṃ kṛtavān?* — "Gopāla, what did you do yesterday?"
2. गोपालः — **अहं पुस्तकं पठितवान्।** — pause: अहम् पुस्तकम् पठितवान्। — *ahaṃ pustakaṃ paṭhitavān.* —
   "I read a book."
3. सीता — **किं पुस्तकं त्वया पठितम्?** — pause: किम् पुस्तकम् त्वया पठितम्? — *kiṃ pustakaṃ tvayā
   paṭhitam?* — "Which book was read by you?"
4. गोपालः — **मया रामायणं पठितम्। रामो वनं गतः, सीतापि वनं गता।** — pause: मया रामायणम् पठितम्। रामः
   वनम् गतः, सीता अपि वनम् गता। — *mayā rāmāyaṇaṃ paṭhitam. rāmo vanaṃ gataḥ, sītāpi vanaṃ gatā.* — "I
   read the Rāmāyaṇa. Rāma went to the forest, and Sītā went to the forest too."
5. सीता — **त्वं विद्यालयं गच्छन्किं पश्यसि?** — pause: त्वम् विद्यालयम् गच्छन् किम् पश्यसि? — *tvaṃ
   vidyālayaṃ gacchan kiṃ paśyasi?* — "What do you see as you go to school?"
6. गोपालः — **विद्यालयं गच्छन्नहं वृक्षान्पश्यामि।** — pause: विद्यालयम् गच्छन् अहम् वृक्षान् पश्यामि। —
   *vidyālayaṃ gacchann ahaṃ vṛkṣān paśyāmi.* — "Going to school, I see trees."
7. सीता — **तव मित्रं कुत्र गतम्?** — pause: तव मित्रम् कुत्र गतम्? — *tava mitraṃ kutra gatam?* — "Where
   has your friend gone?"
8. गोपालः — **मम मित्रं ग्रामं गतम्। तेन मह्यं पत्रं लिखितम्।** — pause: मम मित्रम् ग्रामम् गतम्। तेन
   मह्यम् पत्रम् लिखितम्। — *mama mitraṃ grāmaṃ gatam. tena mahyaṃ patraṃ likhitam.* — "My friend has
   gone to the village. A letter has been written to me by him."
9. सीता — **पत्रे किं लिखितम्?** — pause: पत्रे किम् लिखितम्? — *patre kiṃ likhitam?* — "What is written
   in the letter?"
10. गोपालः — **अहं कुशलीति लिखितम्।** — pause: अहम् कुशली इति लिखितम्। — *ahaṃ kuśalīti likhitam.* —
    "'I am well' is written."
11. सीता — **भद्रम्! अहमपि तस्मै पत्रं लेखिष्यामि।** — pause: भद्रम्! अहम् अपि तस्मै पत्रम् लेखिष्यामि। —
    *bhadram! aham api tasmai patraṃ lekhiṣyāmi.* — "Good! I too will write him a letter."

Seams: ह्यस्त्वम् (visarga → स् before त); गच्छन्नहम् (न् after a short vowel before a vowel → न्न);
सीतापि, कुशलीति (like vowels joined); ṃ; न् before a consonant written together. मित्रम् is neuter
(v2), so गतम्. लेखिष्यामि is लिखति's future (the c03.l05 card).

### §17 Story — the serial, episode 1 (the Pañcatantra's Book 4 frame, line 5261 ff., told in owned words)

1. **मकरो वानरस्य समीपं गतः।** — pause: मकरः वानरस्य समीपम् गतः। — *makaro vānarasya samīpaṃ gataḥ.* —
   "The crocodile has gone to the monkey."
2. **वानरो वृक्षे तिष्ठन्मकरं पश्यति।** — pause: वानरः वृक्षे तिष्ठन् मकरम् पश्यति। — *vānaro vṛkṣe
   tiṣṭhan makaraṃ paśyati.* — "The monkey, sitting in the tree, sees the crocodile."
3. **स हसन्पृच्छति — मित्र, ह्यस्त्वं किमर्थं नागतः?** — pause: सः हसन् पृच्छति — मित्र, ह्यः त्वम्
   किमर्थम् न आगतः? — *sa hasan pṛcchati — mitra, hyas tvaṃ kimarthaṃ nāgataḥ?* — "Laughing, he asks:
   'Friend, why did you not come yesterday?'"
4. **मकरो वदति — मम भार्यया तव कथा श्रुता। अहं त्वां गृहं नेष्यामि।** — pause: मकरः वदति — मम भार्यया
   तव कथा श्रुता। अहम् त्वाम् गृहम् नेष्यामि। — *makaro vadati — mama bhāryayā tava kathā śrutā. ahaṃ
   tvāṃ gṛhaṃ neṣyāmi.* — "The crocodile says: 'My wife has heard your story. I will take you home.'"
5. **वानरः फलं खादन्मकरस्य वचनं शृणोति।** — pause: वानरः फलम् खादन् मकरस्य वचनम् शृणोति। — *vānaraḥ
   phalaṃ khādan makarasya vacanaṃ śṛṇoti.* — "The monkey, eating a fruit, listens to the crocodile's
   words."
6. **किं वानरो मकरेण सह गमिष्यति?** — pause: किम् वानरः मकरेण सह गमिष्यति? — *kiṃ vānaro makareṇa saha
   gamiṣyati?* — "Will the monkey go with the crocodile?"

Questions (keys in §19's key file): (a) Where has the crocodile gone? — to the monkey (वानरस्य समीपम्).
(b) What is the monkey doing while he listens? — eating a fruit (फलं खादन्). The -त forms: गतः, आगतः
(doer agrees: motion), श्रुता (with भार्यया: the thing heard agrees, feminine with कथा).

### §18 Exercises (keys at the end of each)

**Ex 1 (objective 1; 8 items).** (1)–(4) Write the -त and the -तवत् form, masculine nominative
singular: (1) जयति (2) त्यजति (3) नयति (4) शृणोति. (5)–(8) Say the same thing with the other
participle: (5) अहं पत्रं लिखितवान्। → [-त frame] (6) तेन फलं खादितम्। → [-तवत् frame] (7) बालिका गृहं
गता। → [-तवत्] (8) नृपेण कथा कथिता। → [-तवत्].
*Key:* (1) जितः · जितवान् (2) त्यक्तः · त्यक्तवान् (3) नीतः · नीतवान् (4) श्रुतः · श्रुतवान् (5) मया पत्रं
लिखितम्। (6) स फलं खादितवान्। (7) बालिका गृहं गतवती। (8) नृपः कथां कथितवान्। One point per form in
(1)–(4) (8); one per sentence in (5)–(8) (4).

**Ex 2 (objective 2; 6 items).** Join the two sentences, making the first verb a present participle:
(1) बालकः पठति। बालको हसति। (2) नृपो ग्रामं गच्छति। नृपः कथां शृणोति। (3) मकरो जले तिष्ठति। मकरो वानरं
पश्यति। (4) छात्राः पुस्तकानि पठन्ति। छात्रा लिखन्ति। (5) पान्थो धनं लभते। पान्थो हसति। (6) वानरः फलं
खादति। वानरो वृक्षे तिष्ठति।
*Key:* (1) बालकः पठन्हसति। (2) नृपो ग्रामं गच्छन्कथां शृणोति। (3) मकरो जले तिष्ठन्वानरं पश्यति।
(4) छात्राः पुस्तकानि पठन्तो लिखन्ति। (5) पान्थो धनं लभमानो हसति। (6) वानरः फलं खादन्वृक्षे तिष्ठति।
(Each with its pause form in the key.)

**Ex 3 (objective 3; 6 items; derive-it-yourself).** (1) Derive, according to Pāṇini, वच् + क्त →
उक्तम्, in table form, each step with its sūtra. (2) Derive, according to Pāṇini, पठ् + क्त → पठितम्. (3)–(6) According to Pāṇini, for each, say
which job the क् of क्त does — no guṇa · the nasal drops · samprasāraṇa: (3) श्रु → श्रुत (4) मन् → मत
(5) स्वप् → सुप्त (6) हन् → हत.
*Key:* (1) F-c, in full (table form). (2) F-d, in full. (3) no guṇa (1.1.5) (4) the nasal drops (6.4.37;
the Kāśikā names मन्) (5) samprasāraṇa, व् → उ (6.1.15; the Kāśikā's *svapi - suptaḥ*) (6) the nasal
drops (6.4.37; हन् in the Kāśikā's list). D-tier rules by number or gist.

**Ex 4 (objective 4; 6 items).** On N3a, sentence 1 (§13's reading box; the frame line printed
above it: the pigeons ask their king, कथमेतत्? "How was that?", and he tells this story):
(1) Who is speaking, and to whom? (2) Where was the speaker when he saw the tiger? (3) Find the
present participle; who was doing it? (4) What is said of the tiger before he speaks? (5) Find the
-त form that is glossed, and say whom it describes. (6) What does the tiger offer, and to whom?
*Key:* (1) the king of the pigeons (सः), to the pigeons (2) in the southern forest, wandering
(दक्षिणारण्ये चरन्) (3) चरन् — the speaker himself (अहम्) (4) he is old, bathed, with kuśa-grass in his
hand, on the shore of a lake (वृद्धः, स्नातः, कुशहस्तः, सरस्तीरे) (5) स्नातः — the tiger (6) a gold bracelet
(सुवर्णकङ्कणम्), to the travellers (पान्थाः).

**The reading box (§13, after the model sentences).** N3a sentence 1 as table 0.J prints it — joined,
pause form, IAST, English — with its four glosses in the margin, the frame line above it, and the
source line: "Hitopadeśa 1, kathā 1, the prose after 1.5; text of the GRETIL e-text
[REF:hitopadesha 1, kathā 1]". The printing changes are listed in Teacher Notes (table 0.J).

### §19 Quiz (10 items; the correct option is listed first in this fact base; the lesson orders
the options so that `scripts/quizcheck.py` passes, and no explanation names an option by position)

1. [FACT] मया पुस्तकं ___। — **पठितम्** · पठितः · पठितवान्
2. [FACT] सीता वनं ___। — **गता** · गतः · गतम्
3. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, what does the क् of क्त do in कृतः? — **it blocks guṇa of the
   root's ऋ** · it brings the augment इट् · it takes away the root's nasal
4. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, why is there no म् in गतः? — **the nasal of गम् drops before a
   k-marked affix beginning with a jhal** · व् becomes उ before a k-marked affix · guṇa is blocked
5. [FACT] Which word says "he did (it)", the doer in the nominative? — **कृतवान्** · कृतम् · करिष्यति
6. [FACT] बालको ग्रामं ___ हसति। "The boy laughs as he goes to the village." — **गच्छन्** · गतः · गमिष्यति
7. [FACT] The present participle of लभते, masculine nominative singular: — **लभमानः** · लब्धः · लब्धवान्
8. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, शतृ and शानच् stand in place of — **laṭ, the l-affix of the
   present** · the root · the class sign
9. [FACT] In the reading, चरन्नपश्यम् — who was wandering? — **the speaker (अहम्)** · the tiger · the
   travellers
10. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, why does उक्तः begin with उ? — **before a k-marked affix वच् takes
    samprasāraṇa: व् → उ** · the क् blocks guṇa · the nasal of the root drops

Every option is a real, owned form or a statement; no ◐ form and no non-form is offered (Q6's
गमिष्यति is the c03.l05 future, ✓).

## 2. L2 — क्त्वा तुमुन् तृच्: Having Done, To Do, the Doer — the Absolutive, the Infinitive and the Agent (grammar)

**Front matter.** id `v4.c04.l02` · file `lesson-02-having-done-to-do-the-doer.md` · title sa क्त्वा
तुमुन् तृच् · sa_iast *ktvā tumun tṛc* · en "Having Done, To Do, the Doer: the Absolutive, the
Infinitive and the Agent" · hi "करके, करने के लिए, करने वाला: पूर्वकालिक कृदन्त, तुमुन् और कर्ता" ·
lesson_type grammar · cefr B1 · duration 45 · self_study_minutes **48** · prerequisites [v4.c04.l01] ·
review_of [v4.c04.l01, v4.c03.l06, v3.c06.l01, v3.c06.l02, v2.c05.l03] · vocabulary_count **7** · tags
[grammar, panini, krt, absolutive, ktva, lyap, tumun, infinitive, agent-noun, k-marker, avyaya,
hitopadesha, reading, serial, spaced-review] · media (planned): image `v4c04l02-having-done.svg` (two
actions on a time line with one doer above both, the first labelled -त्वा, the second the finite
verb; beside it the same root with -तुम्, its full grade shown); audio `v4c04l02-derivation-read.mp3`,
`v4c04l02-n3a-read.mp3`, `v4c04l02-conversation.mp3`; worksheet `v4c04l02-absolutives.pdf`; quizzes
`v4c04l02-quiz.md`, `v4c04l02-quiz-key.md`.

**Objectives** (one exercise each):
1. en "Join two actions of one doer with -त्वा, or -य after a prefix, and test whether a pair has one
   doer." · hi "एक ही कर्ता की दो क्रियाओं को -त्वा से, या उपसर्ग के बाद -य से, जोड़ें, और जाँचें कि किसी
   जोड़े का कर्ता एक है या नहीं।" (Ex 1, 8 items)
2. en "Form -तुम् for the purpose of another action, and with इच्छति and शक्नोति." · hi "जिस
   क्रिया के लिए कोई दूसरी क्रिया की जाती है, उसका -तुम् रूप बनाएँ, और इच्छति तथा शक्नोति के साथ भी।" (Ex 2, 6
   items)
3. en "Derive, according to Pāṇini, आगत्य with the teacher and गत्वा and गन्तुम् by yourself, and say
   what the क् of क्त्वा does that तुमुन् and तृच् lack." · hi "पाणिनि के अनुसार शिक्षक के साथ आगत्य की, और
   स्वयं गत्वा और गन्तुम् की व्युत्पत्ति करें; और बताएँ कि क्त्वा का क् क्या करता है जो तुमुन् और तृच् में
   नहीं होता।" (Ex 3, 6 items)
4. en "Form the agent noun in -तृ of six card verbs, in the cells of दाता." · hi "कार्ड की छह क्रियाओं से
   -तृ वाला कर्ता-शब्द बनाएँ, दाता के रूपों की तरह।" (Ex 4, 6 items)

**The opening (§1).** Volume 3's R5 card said of उक्त्वा and आगत्य: "you read it, you find its doer, you
never build it" (v3.c06.l01 §8). Today you build it. Question:
why does गत्वा lose the म् of गम् while गन्तुम् keeps its nasal (as न्)?

**The one grammar point.** Two kṛt forms that do not decline: -त्वा (क्त्वा) for the earlier of two
actions with one doer (3.4.21), -य (ल्यप्) in its place after a prefix (7.1.37); -तुम् (तुमुन्) for the
purpose of another action (3.3.10). क्त्वा is k-marked like क्त; तुमुन् and तृच् are not —
so कृत्वा but कर्तुम्, कर्ता.

### §3 Station 0 (9 minutes; the class plan's first nine)

>   runs exactly this. **(3 min)**
> - +1 week: class detective — for तिष्ठति, जायते, पृच्छति, शृणोति, अस्ति, ब्रवीति, युनक्ति, जानाति,
>   चिन्तयति and स्वपिति name the class (by its first root), the class sign and the present stem, and
>   give the rule of each one whose stem replaces the root, with the substitute-stem card open;
>   v4.c04.l02's Station 0 runs exactly this. **(5 min)**
> - Prompts (unscored, 1 min): उक्त्वा, आगत्य — what do they mean? (v3.c06.l01) · एवमुक्त्वा — take it
>   apart (v3.c06.l02) · say "I want water" (v2.c05.l03)

Key (✓ only): S0-fa and S0-el as table 0.I keys them (7.3.79's stems read off the card, not
scored); prompts: "having said", "having come" · एवम् + उक्त्वा, "having said so" · अहं जलमिच्छामि. §3
reaches v4.c04.l01 (item 1), v4.c03.l06 (item 2), v3.c06.l01, v3.c06.l02, v2.c05.l03 (prompts).

### §4 Callouts

- **TRADITION.** According to Pāṇini, क्त्वा comes after a root when two actions have one doer, for
  the earlier one (3.4.21 [Q]); the Kāśikā's counter-examples, in English (their Sanskrit, K14, stands in Teacher Notes): with two
  doers — "Devadatta goes when the brahmin has eaten" — and with no "earlier" — "he walks and talks".
  [REF:ashtadhyayi 3.4.21]; [REF:kashika 3.4.21]
- **FACT.** Whitney: the gerund "accompanying or (more often) a preceding action"; with a prefix the
  form in य (§§989–990); "A root ending in a short vowel takes त्य tya" (§992); and the infinitive's
  root "has the guṇa-strengthening" (§968). [REF:whitney1889 §989]; §990; §992; §968
- **FACT.** The agent noun in -तृ: "the root itself being strengthened by guṇa … the roots in ṛ take
  no i: thus, kartṛ (against kariṣya); roots han and gam show the same difference". [REF:whitney1889
  §943]

### §5 What the lesson corrects (plain text)

The 1.x page restores its own lesson (pp. 106–108, "Gerund & Infinitive") with two corrections and
two omissions, each quoted from the page image (X6–X8): (1) "After a prefix (other than नञ्),
क्त्वा becomes ल्यप्" — the sūtra says "in a compound whose first member is not नञ्": नञ् is the
negative अ-, not a prefix (the Kāśikā's अकृत्वा), and a prefix and root make a compound by 2.2.18
(lesson 4); (2) रामः ग्रामं गन्तुम् इच्छति, printed under 3.3.10, rests on another rule — the -तुम् with
a verb of wishing (Teacher Notes) — while सः वेदं पठितुम् आगच्छति is 3.3.10's own kind; (3) a chain of absolutives
before one verb: the Kāśikā's own example under 3.4.21 strings three (*snātvā pītvā bhuktvā vrajati*,
with the listed correction), and adds that the rule's dual does not limit the chain to two.

### §6–§7 Sanskrit and Hindi explanation

§6, two lines, joined, pause beneath:
1. **स गृहं गत्वा खादति। पूर्वं गच्छति, पश्चात्खादति।** — pause: सः गृहम् गत्वा खादति। पूर्वम् गच्छति,
   पश्चात् खादति। — "He goes home and eats. First he goes, afterwards he eats." (One doer, the earlier
   action in -त्वा.)
2. **स स्नातुं गच्छति, स्नात्वा च गृहमागच्छति।** — pause: सः स्नातुम् गच्छति, स्नात्वा च गृहम् आगच्छति। —
   "He goes to bathe, and having bathed he comes home." (-तुम्: what the going is for.)
Seams: सः → स before a consonant; त् + ख written together; ṃ; m + vowel joined.
§7 (Hindi): the Hindi -कर / -के (जाकर, करके) and -ने (जाने के लिए) as glosses of the headings; the
Hindi school name पूर्वकालिक कृदन्त as GLOSSARY prints it (row क्त्वा · ल्यप्); no FACT callout.

### §8 English explanation — the steps

**Step 1 — one doer, the earlier action.** Q — **3.4.21 समानकर्तृकयोः पूर्वकाले** [Q]. Padaccheda, as
the data file gives it: समानकर्त्तृकयोः (सप्तमी, dual) · पूर्वकाले (सप्तमी, singular) — the file spells
the first word -र्त्तृ-, with a doubled त् after र् (Monier-Williams prints *kartṛ* and *samāna-kartṛka*, Apte *kartṛ* and *kartṛka*, each with one t); the sūtra text
has -र्तृ-. Here the two locatives do not mean "before" (1.1.66): they name the circumstance, as the
Kāśikā reads them — "of two root-meanings with the same doer, in the earlier one" (K14). Bracket line
(0.H): [carried (data file): कृत्, 3.1.93; क्त्वा, 3.4.18] — the affix itself is carried from 3.4.18.
English: "[After a root,] when two actions have the same doer, [क्त्वा] for the earlier." The test:
can one doer be named for both verbs? मकरो वानरं दृष्ट्वा वदति — yes; \*मयि आगत्य स गच्छति — no (two
doers; c05.l04 gives that pattern its own construction).

**Step 2 — -त्वा, and what its क् does.** क्त्वा → -त्वा (क् a marker, 1.3.8, 1.3.9 [B]). It is k-marked,
exactly as क्त was: so -त्वा takes the root in the shape -त takes (Whitney §991: with regard to the auxiliary i, "as well as to the form of the root before it, the
formation nearly agrees with that of the participle in ta"): कृत्वा, गत्वा, उक्त्वा, दृष्ट्वा, श्रुत्वा;
दा → दत्त्वा. An avyaya: D — **1.1.40 क्त्वातोसुन्कसुनः** [D] (bracket: अव्ययम्, 1.1.37) — "A word ending
in क्त्वा, तोसुन् or कसुन् is called avyaya." Its ending is then deleted (2.4.82 [D, owned]).

**Step 3 — after a prefix: -य.** D — **7.1.37 समासेऽनञ्पूर्वे क्त्वो ल्यप्** [D] (bracket: अन्यतरस्याम्,
7.1.35 — the file carries it; this rule does not use it) — "In a compound whose first member is not
नञ्, क्त्वा is replaced by ल्यप्." The Kāśikā's pairs: कृत्वा (no compound) against प्रकृत्य; अकृत्वा (नञ्)
against प्रकृत्य. Why आ + गम्-क्त्वा is a compound: a prefix compounds with a kṛt-final word, 2.2.18
(lesson 4) — a marked line in F-f. D — **6.4.38 वा ल्यपि** [D] — "Before ल्यप् the nasal loss [of 6.4.37]
is optional for a root in म्; for the others the loss is obligatory" (the Kāśikā's *vyavasthitavibhāṣā*, K18) — so for गम्, either: आगत्य or आगम्य (the Kāśikā's own pair); D — **6.1.71 ह्रस्वस्य
पिति कृति तुक्** [D] — "After a short vowel, before a p-marked kṛt, the augment तुक्": आगत्य, उपकृत्य; not
after a long vowel: प्रदाय. With the teacher: F-f, both routes. ल्यप् counts as a kṛt as क्त्वा did —
a labelled step (1.1.56, c06.l03).

**Step 4 — -तुम्, the infinitive.** D — **3.3.10 तुमुन्ण्वुलौ क्रियायां क्रियार्थायाम्** [D] (bracket:
भविष्यति, 3.3.3, by name) — "After a root that names the purpose of another action, तुमुन् (or
ण्वुल्)": सः स्नातुं गच्छति — the going is for the bathing. With इच्छति and शक्नोति (one doer), the -तुम्
rests on two more rules (Teacher Notes): अहं गन्तुमिच्छामि, स वक्तुं न शक्नोति. D — **1.1.39 कृन्मेजन्तः**
[D] — "A kṛt ending in म् or in an एच् vowel is called avyaya": -तुम् ends in म्. Two sound rules for
गन्तुम् (D — **8.3.24 नश्चापदान्तस्य झलि** [D]: "न् or म् not at the end of a pada becomes anusvāra before
a jhal"; D — **8.4.58 अनुस्वारस्य ययि परसवर्णः** [D]: "anusvāra before a yay sound becomes the nasal of
that sound's row"): गम्तुम् → गंतुम् → गन्तुम्. The learner's derivations, गत्वा and गन्तुम्, are Ex 3
(1)–(2).

**Step 5 — the k-marker's three jobs, in pairs.** क्त्वा has क्; तुमुन् and तृच् have none. Minimal
pairs, each with its job named: कृत्वा / कर्तुम्, कर्ता (guṇa blocked by 1.1.5, given by 7.3.84 where no
क् stops it); गत्वा / गन्तुम्, गन्ता (the nasal lost, 6.4.37); उक्त्वा / वक्तुम्, वक्ता (samprasāraṇa,
6.1.15); दृष्ट्वा / द्रष्टुम्, द्रष्टा (without क् दृश् takes an augment, Teacher Notes).

**Step 6 — the doer in -तृ.** The same full root with -तृ names the doer: कर्ता, गन्ता, वक्ता, द्रष्टा,
श्रोता, दाता, प्रवेष्टा (Whitney §943; Monier-Williams for each stem). Its cells are दाता's in T5
(v3.c02.l04): वक्ता, वक्तारम्, वक्तारः … No rule number is given for its affix in this lesson: the
learner meets that rule unseen in the Volume 4 exam.

### §9–§11 Pronunciation, IPA, mouth

Drill: गत्वा /gɐt̪ʋɑː/ · गन्तुम् /gɐn̪t̪um/ · गन्ता /gɐn̪t̪ɑː/ · कृत्वा /kr̩t̪ʋɑː/ · कर्तुम् /kɐɾt̪um/ · उक्त्वा
/ukt̪ʋɑː/ · वक्तुम् /ʋɐkt̪um/ · दृष्ट्वा /d̪r̩ʂʈʋɑː/ · द्रष्टुम् /d̪ɾɐʂʈum/ · आगत्य /ɑːgɐt̪jɐ/ · आगम्य /ɑːgɐmjɐ/
· प्रदाय /pɾɐd̪ɑːjɐ/. Mouth: in -त्वा the lower lip rises to the upper teeth for व, lips unrounded, while the tongue is still at
the teeth for त;
in गन्तुम् the anusvāra is not a separate sound — the tongue is already at the teeth for न (8.4.58 in
the mouth).

### §12 Common mistakes (six rows)

| Mistake | Fix | Why |
|---|---|---|
| \*मयि आगत्य स गच्छति | मयि आगते स गच्छति — c05.l04; today: say two sentences | -त्वा/-य needs one doer for both actions (3.4.21) |
| \*गन्त्वा | गत्वा | the nasal drops before the k-marked क्त्वा (6.4.37) |
| \*वच्त्वा | उक्त्वा | samprasāraṇa before a k-marked affix (6.1.15) |
| \*आगत्वा | आगत्य | after a prefix (in a compound), -य (7.1.37) |
| \*कृतुम् | कर्तुम् | तुमुन् has no क्: guṇa (7.3.84) |
| \*प्रदत्य | प्रदाय | तुक् only after a short vowel (6.1.71) |

### §13 Examples — model sentences

1. **स गृहं गत्वा पश्चाद्भोजनं खादति।** — pause: सः गृहम् गत्वा पश्चात् भोजनम् खादति। — *sa gṛhaṃ gatvā
   paścād bhojanaṃ khādati.* — "He goes home and afterwards eats."
2. **मूषक आगत्य जालमकृन्तत्।** — pause: मूषकः आगत्य जालम् अकृन्तत्। — *mūṣaka āgatya jālam akṛntat.* —
   "The mouse came and cut the net."
3. **व्याघ्रो जले स्नात्वा पान्थं वदति।** — pause: व्याघ्रः जले स्नात्वा पान्थम् वदति। — *vyāghro jale
   snātvā pānthaṃ vadati.* — "The tiger, having bathed in the water, speaks to the traveller."
4. **पङ्कात्स निर्गन्तुं न शक्नोति।** — pause: पङ्कात् सः निर्गन्तुम् न शक्नोति। — *paṅkāt sa nirgantuṃ na
   śaknoti.* — "He cannot get out of the mud." (Re-worded from the plan's स पङ्कान्निर्गन्तुं…: त् + न → न्न
   is a ◐ seam; §7, deviation 4.)
5. **तद्वचनमाकर्ण्य पान्थो लोभात्सरः प्राविशत्।** — pause: तत् वचनम् आकर्ण्य पान्थः लोभात् सरः प्राविशत्। —
   *tad vacanam ākarṇya pāntho lobhāt saraḥ prāviśat.* — "Hearing those words, the traveller out of
   greed entered the lake."
6. **कर्ता करोति, वक्ता वदति। किं कर्तुमिच्छसीत्यालोचय।** — pause: कर्ता करोति, वक्ता वदति। किम् कर्तुम्
   इच्छसि इति आलोचय। — *kartā karoti, vaktā vadati. kiṃ kartum icchasīty ālocaya.* — "The doer does, the
   speaker speaks. Consider what you want to do." (The plan's semicolon split into two sentences:
   the course's stops have no semicolon; §7, deviation 6.)

Seams: त् → द् before a voiced sound (पश्चाद्भोजनम्, तद्वचनम्); the visarga dropped before a vowel other
than अ (मूषक आगत्य); ओ for अः (व्याघ्रो, पान्थो); m + vowel joined; i + vowel → y + vowel (इच्छसीत्यालोचय:
इ + इ → ई, then इ + आ → या); त् + स, त् + प written together. निर्गन्तुम्: निर्गच्छति's -तुम्, निस् + गन्तुम्.
प्राविशत्: the imperfect of प्रविशति (Volume 3). वदति with the person spoken to in the accusative:
Apte "to speak to", and the Hitopadeśa's *taṃ maṇḍūka-nātham avadat* (line 3813).

**The reading box.** N3a sentence 2, as table 0.J prints it — joined, pause, IAST, English — with
one gloss (लोभाकृष्टेन) and the source line "Hitopadeśa 1, kathā 1, the prose after 1.5; text of the
GRETIL e-text [REF:hitopadesha 1, kathā 1]". Two questions, unscored, in class: who thought, and what
did he think? (a traveller drawn by greed; "this comes about by luck"). आलोचितम् is the -त of today's
आलोचयति: "it was considered by …" — the frame of l01 (मया पठितम्).
