> T5 makes ✓ for दाता; स्नाति, आकर्णयति and आलोचयति in laṭ P, शक्नोति in laṭ P singular.

### §15 Grammar — the cards

1. **The k-marker card** (l01's, with two new columns: -त्वा and -तुम्, -तृ) — the four pairs of Step 5.
2. **The kṛt card**, table 0.A columns 5–8 (the plan's ten-verb table, with its checks: स्नाता and
   आलोचयितुम् not printed for want of a checked form, §7 deviation 7).
3. **The absolutive's choice:** no prefix → -त्वा (गत्वा, कृत्वा); a prefix → -य (आगत्य/आगम्य, उपकृत्य,
   प्रविश्य, प्रदाय, आलोच्य [its णिच् dropped: Teacher Notes], आकर्ण्य); a short root vowel before -य → -त्य
   (आगत्य, उपकृत्य).
4. **The same-doer test** (Step 1), with the Kāśikā's two counter-examples in English (their Sanskrit in Teacher Notes).
5. **Paradigm ledger at exit of l02:**

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| -त्वा and -य/-त्य of the card's verbs | ✓ |
| -तुम्: of purpose (3.3.10), and with इच्छति, शक्नोति | ✓ |
| -तृ: the card's agent nouns, in T5's ✓ cells | ✓ |
| the -अम् absolutive (e.g. the Kāśikā's स्वादुङ्कारम्) | ◐ (Teacher Notes) |
| the gerundive (कर्तव्यम्, करणीयम्: the Kāśikā's examples under 3.1.93) | ○ (c05.l01) |
| शक्नोति's plural (शक्नुवन्ति) | ◐ |

### §16 Conversation (ten turns; homework)

Two friends, सीता and गोपालः, planning the next day.

1. सीता — **श्वः किं कर्तुमिच्छसि?** — pause: श्वः किम् कर्तुम् इच्छसि? — *śvaḥ kiṃ kartum icchasi?* — "What
   do you want to do tomorrow?"
2. गोपालः — **अहं नगरं गन्तुमिच्छामि।** — pause: अहम् नगरम् गन्तुम् इच्छामि। — *ahaṃ nagaraṃ gantum
   icchāmi.* — "I want to go to the city."
3. सीता — **नगरं गत्वा किं करिष्यसि?** — pause: नगरम् गत्वा किम् करिष्यसि? — *nagaraṃ gatvā kiṃ
   kariṣyasi?* — "Having gone to the city, what will you do?"
4. गोपालः — **नगरं गत्वा मम मित्रं द्रक्ष्यामि। तेन सहापणं गमिष्यामि।** — pause: नगरम् गत्वा मम मित्रम्
   द्रक्ष्यामि। तेन सह आपणम् गमिष्यामि। — *nagaraṃ gatvā mama mitraṃ drakṣyāmi. tena sahāpaṇaṃ
   gamiṣyāmi.* — "In the city I will see my friend. With him I will go to the market."
5. सीता — **किं त्वं मार्गं जानासि?** — pause: किम् त्वम् मार्गम् जानासि? — *kiṃ tvaṃ mārgaṃ jānāsi?* — "Do
   you know the way?"
6. गोपालः — **न जानामि। पश्चात्पृच्छामि।** — pause: न जानामि। पश्चात् पृच्छामि। — *na jānāmi. paścāt
   pṛcchāmi.* — "I don't. I'll ask afterwards."
7. सीता — **अहमप्यागन्तुं शक्नोमि?** — pause: अहम् अपि आगन्तुम् शक्नोमि? — *aham apy āgantuṃ śaknomi?* —
   "Can I come too?"
8. गोपालः — **आम्। प्रभाते स्नात्वा मम गृहमागच्छ।** — pause: आम्। प्रभाते स्नात्वा मम गृहम् आगच्छ। —
   *ām. prabhāte snātvā mama gṛham āgaccha.* — "Yes. In the morning, after your bath, come to my
   house."
9. सीता — **भद्रम्। तव गृहमागत्य त्वया सह गमिष्यामि।** — pause: भद्रम्। तव गृहम् आगत्य त्वया सह
   गमिष्यामि। — *bhadram. tava gṛham āgatya tvayā saha gamiṣyāmi.* — "Good. I will come to your house and
   go with you."
10. गोपालः — **भद्रम्! श्वो मिलामः।** — pause: भद्रम्! श्वः मिलामः। — *bhadram! śvo milāmaḥ.* — "Good! We
    meet tomorrow."

Seams: सहापणम् (अ + आ → आ); अप्यागन्तुम् (इ + आ → या); पश्चात्पृच्छामि (त् + प); श्वो मिलामः (अः → ओ
before म); m + vowel joined. द्रक्ष्यामि is पश्यति's future (the c03.l05 card).

### §17 Story — the serial, episode 2 (the Pañcatantra frame, lines 5277–5281, in owned words)

1. **वानरो मकरस्य वचनमाकर्ण्य चिन्तयति।** — pause: वानरः मकरस्य वचनम् आकर्ण्य चिन्तयति। — *vānaro
   makarasya vacanam ākarṇya cintayati.* — "Hearing the crocodile's words, the monkey thinks."
2. **स वदति — मित्र, अहं जले गन्तुं न शक्नोमि। कथं तव गृहं गमिष्यामि?** — pause: सः वदति — मित्र, अहम्
   जले गन्तुम् न शक्नोमि। कथम् तव गृहम् गमिष्यामि? — *sa vadati — mitra, ahaṃ jale gantuṃ na śaknomi.
   kathaṃ tava gṛhaṃ gamiṣyāmi?* — "He says: 'Friend, I cannot go in the water. How shall I go to
   your house?'"
3. **मकरो वदति — मम पृष्ठमारुह्य सुखेन गच्छ।** — pause: मकरः वदति — मम पृष्ठम् आरुह्य सुखेन गच्छ। —
   *makaro vadati — mama pṛṣṭham āruhya sukhena gaccha.* — "The crocodile says: 'Climb onto my back and
   go in comfort.'" Glossed: पृष्ठम् "back", आरुह्य "having climbed".
4. **वानरः सम्यगालोच्य मकरस्य पृष्ठमारुह्योपविशति।** — pause: वानरः सम्यक् आलोच्य मकरस्य पृष्ठम् आरुह्य
   उपविशति। — *vānaraḥ samyag ālocya makarasya pṛṣṭham āruhyopaviśati.* — "The monkey, having thought it
   over well, climbs onto the crocodile's back and sits down."
5. **मकरो जलं प्रविश्य शीघ्रं गच्छति।** — pause: मकरः जलम् प्रविश्य शीघ्रम् गच्छति। — *makaro jalaṃ
   praviśya śīghraṃ gacchati.* — "The crocodile enters the water and goes fast."
6. **वानरो जलं दृष्ट्वा पृच्छति — मित्र, तव गृहं कुत्र?** — pause: वानरः जलम् दृष्ट्वा पृच्छति — मित्र, तव
   गृहम् कुत्र? — *vānaro jalaṃ dṛṣṭvā pṛcchati — mitra, tava gṛhaṃ kutra?* — "Seeing the water, the
   monkey asks: 'Friend, where is your home?'"

Questions: (a) What did the monkey do first — think it over, or climb? (thought it over: आलोच्य, then
climbed: आरुह्य) (b) Why does the monkey say he cannot go? (he cannot go in the water: जले गन्तुं न
शक्नोमि). Seams: सम्यगालोच्य (क् → ग् before a vowel); आरुह्योपविशति (अ + उ → ओ); शीघ्रं गच्छति (ṃ
before a consonant). Glossed: 2.

### §18 Exercises

**Ex 1 (objective 1; 8 items).** (1)–(6) Join into one sentence with -त्वा, or -य after a prefix:
(1) छात्रः कार्यं करोति। छात्रः स्वपिति। (2) नृपो वचनमाकर्णयति। नृपो हसति। (3) मकरो जलं प्रविशति। मकरो
गच्छति। (4) पान्थो धनं गृह्णाति। पान्थो गच्छति। (5) माता गृहमागच्छति। माता भोजनं पचति। (6) वानर आलोचयति।
वानरो वदति। (7)–(8) Can the pair be joined with an absolutive? Say why, and join it if it can be:
(7) रामो ग्रामं गच्छति। सीता हसति। (8) मकरो वानरं पश्यति। मकरो हसति।
*Key:* (1) छात्रः कार्यं कृत्वा स्वपिति। (2) नृपो वचनमाकर्ण्य हसति। (3) मकरो जलं प्रविश्य गच्छति।
(4) पान्थो धनं गृहीत्वा गच्छति। (5) माता गृहमागत्य भोजनं पचति। [आगम्य accepted] (6) वानर आलोच्य वदति।
(7) No: two doers (राम, सीता); 3.4.21 needs one. (8) Yes: मकरो वानरं दृष्ट्वा हसति।

**Ex 2 (objective 2; 6 items).** Complete with the -तुम् form of the verb in brackets: (1) पान्थः सरः
___ गच्छति। [स्ना] (2) अहं कथां ___ इच्छामि। [श्रु] (3) स किमपि ___ न शक्नोति। [वच्] (4) बालिका मित्रं ___
आगच्छति। [दृश्] (5) नृपो धनं ___ इच्छति। [दा] (6) वानरो जलं ___ न शक्नोति। [प्रविश्]
*Key:* (1) स्नातुम् (2) श्रोतुम् (3) वक्तुम् (4) द्रष्टुम् (5) दातुम् (6) प्रवेष्टुम्; before a consonant the joined
स्नातुं, वक्तुं, प्रवेष्टुं score the same.

**Ex 3 (objective 3; 6 items; derive-it-yourself).** (1) Derive, according to Pāṇini, गम् + क्त्वा →
गत्वा in table form. (2) Derive, according to Pāṇini, गम् + तुमुन् → गन्तुम्. (3)–(6) According to Pāṇini, for each pair, name the k-marker's job,
or say that the second affix has no क्: (3) कृत्वा / कर्तुम् (4) गत्वा / गन्तुम् (5) उक्त्वा / वक्तुम्
(6) श्रुत्वा / श्रोता.
*Key:* (1) F-g (2) F-h (3) the क् of क्त्वा blocks guṇa; तुमुन् has none: कर्- (4) the nasal drops before
the k-marked क्त्वा; not before तुमुन् (5) samprasāraṇa before क्त्वा; none before तुमुन् (6) no guṇa
before क्त्वा; guṇa before तृच्, which has no क्: श्रो-.

**Ex 4 (objective 4; 6 items).** Give the agent noun: (1) one who speaks [वच्] (2) one who sees [दृश्]
(3) one who hears [श्रु] (4) one who goes [गम्] — each in the nominative singular; (5) "the speakers"
(nominative plural) (6) "the hearer" (accusative singular: त्वं ___ पश्यसि).
*Key:* (1) वक्ता (2) द्रष्टा (3) श्रोता (4) गन्ता (5) वक्तारः (6) श्रोतारम्.

### §19 Quiz (correct option listed first; ordering by `quizcheck.py` in the lesson)

1. [FACT] मकरो वानरं ___ वदति। "Having seen the monkey, the crocodile speaks." — **दृष्ट्वा** · दृष्टः ·
   द्रष्टुम्
2. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini (3.4.21), क्त्वा is used — **for the earlier of two actions that have
   one doer** · for any past action · for the later of two actions
3. [FACT] मूषक ___ जालमकृन्तत्। "The mouse came and cut the net." — **आगत्य** · आगतः · आगन्तुम्
4. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, आगत्य ends in -य because — **in a compound whose first member is not नञ्,
   क्त्वा is replaced by ल्यप्** · its root is गम् · its two actions have two doers
5. [FACT] अहं नगरं ___ इच्छामि। — **गन्तुम्** · गत्वा · गन्ता
6. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, why is there guṇa in कर्तुम् but not in कृत्वा? — **तुमुन् has no क्,
   and क्त्वा is k-marked** · कर्तुम् is a future form · कृत्वा has a prefix
7. [FACT] The agent noun of वच्, nominative singular — **वक्ता** · उक्तः · वक्तुम्
8. [FACT] स जले ___ न शक्नोति। "He cannot bathe in the water." — **स्नातुम्** · स्नात्वा · स्नातः
9. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, -त्वा and -तुम् words do not decline because — **their rules name
   them avyaya** · they are verbs · they are compounds
10. [FACT] In the reading, who thought "This comes about by luck"? — **a traveller, drawn by greed** ·
    the tiger · the king of the pigeons

## 3. L3 — तद्धिताः: Words from Words — the Secondary Suffixes (grammar)

**Objectives:**
1. en "Make the -ता or -त्व word, the -मत्/-वत् word and the -इन् word of owned bases in the
   nominative, and say what each means." · hi "परिचित शब्दों से -ता या -त्व, -मत्/-वत् और -इन् वाले शब्द
   प्रथमा में बनाएँ और उनका अर्थ बताएँ।" (Ex 1, 8 items)
2. en "Say for twelve derivatives whether the first vowel took vṛddhi, and why." · hi "बारह व्युत्पन्न
   शब्दों के लिए बताएँ कि पहले स्वर को वृद्धि हुई या नहीं, और क्यों।" (Ex 2, 12 items)
3. en "Derive, according to Pāṇini, पाणिनीयम् and लौकिकम् with the teacher and एकता and बलवान् by
   yourself." · hi "पाणिनि के अनुसार शिक्षक के साथ पाणिनीयम् और लौकिकम् की, और स्वयं एकता और बलवान् की
   व्युत्पत्ति करें।" (Ex 3, 6 items)
4. en "Use the card's -तर and -तम forms and the pair यावत् … तावत् in sentences." · hi "कार्ड के -तर और
   -तम रूपों और यावत् … तावत् जोड़ी का वाक्यों में प्रयोग करें।" (Ex 4, 6 items)

**The opening (§1).** In its first weeks the course gave लौकिक "worldly" (v1.c02.l05), एकता "oneness"
(v1.c02.l04) and ममता "mine-ness" (v1.c02.l01) as whole words; Volume 3 read पक्षी "bird" (R1,
v3.c02.l03) and श्रीमान् (R6, v3.c05.l01); Volume 3's three verses read पात्रता and पात्रत्व whole,
"the formation is v4.c04's" (v3.c06.l04 §8). And this volume's own name, पाणिनीयम्. Question: what
do the -ता of एकता, the -ई of पक्षी and the औ of लौकिक have in common?

**The one grammar point.** A taddhita is added after a word (a nominal base), not a root (4.1.76), in
a sense its rule names — "the being of", "who has it", "known in", "descendant of". A ñ- or ṇ-marked
taddhita gives the base's first vowel vṛddhi (7.2.117); before a taddhita beginning with a vowel or
य् the base is bha (1.4.18) and its final इ or अ drops (6.4.148).

>   पान्थो भोजनं खादति। (6) माता पुत्राय फलं ददाति। माता गृहं गच्छति। v4.c04.l03's Station 0 runs

Key (✓ only): S0-fc and S0-fb as table 0.I; prompts: आ ऐ औ (1.1.1) · vṛddhi of the penultimate अ —
named by its gist · worldly; oneness; mine-ness · bird; glorious (a title of respect). §3 reaches
every lesson of review_of.

### §4 Callouts

- **TRADITION.** According to Pāṇini, the affixes taught from 4.1.76 to the end of Book 5 are called
  taddhita (the Kāśikā: *āpañcamādhyāyaparisamāpteḥ*, K24). [REF:ashtadhyayi 4.1.76]; [REF:kashika
  4.1.76]
- **FACT.** Whitney on secondary derivation: "Before a suffix beginning with a vowel or with y … final
  a- and i-vowels are regularly lost" (§1203a); the vṛddhi of the first syllable is a mark of many
  secondary derivatives (§1204); -tā and -tva make abstract nouns, "mamátā selfishness" among them
  (§1237c, §1239); -in is made "almost unlimitedly from stems in a" — *dhanín, pakṣín, balín* (§1230).
  [REF:whitney1889 §1203]; §1204; §1230; §1237; §1239
- **TRADITION.** According to the Kāśikā, पाणिनीयम् is "taught by Pāṇini" (*proktam*), "not made"
  (*na tu kṛtam*) (a rule in Teacher Notes; K38) — so the lesson says "taught by", never "made by".
  [REF:kashika 4.3.101]

### §5 What the lesson explains (plain text)

Why some taddhitas change the first vowel and others do not: the marker. ठञ् and अञ् carry ञ्, अण् carries
ण् — vṛddhi (लौकिक; दैवम्, whose अञ् a vārttika gives, Teacher Notes); तल्, त्व, मतुप्, इनि, छ carry neither — no vṛddhi (एकता, not \*ऐकता;
पक्षी). A plain -क (बालकः, owned) brings none either: its क् is the affix's own sound, not a marker,
because 1.3.8 [B] exempts a taddhita ("at the beginning of an affix that is not a taddhita") — the
two facts the plan's review asked for together (§7.5 restate-2#11). The -तः of ततः, इतः, अतः, कुतः
(owned since v1.c02.l06, whose "suffix story" this is) is a taddhita too, on the card (◐).

### §6–§7 Sanskrit and Hindi explanation

§6, two lines:
1. **तद्धितः शब्दात्परः, न धातोः।** — pause: तद्धितः शब्दात् परः, न धातोः। — "A taddhita comes after a word,
   not after a root."
2. **एकस्य भाव एकता, बलमस्यास्तीति बली।** — pause: एकस्य भावः एकता, बलम् अस्य अस्ति इति बली। — "The
   being of one: एकता; 'he has strength': बली." (भावः glossed "being"; a line joining लोक and लौकिक was
   set aside at drafting, its seam त् + ल being ◐.)
Seams: अः before a vowel other than अ drops its visarga (भाव एकता); m + vowel joined; अ + अ → आ; इ + इ → ई.
§7 (Hindi): Hindi's own abstract -ता (एकता, ममता — the same words) is noted as the Hindi gloss of the
headings; no FACT callout (row 0.17).

### §8 English explanation — the steps

**Step 1 — taddhita.** D — **4.1.76 तद्धिताः** [D] (bracket: अनुपसर्जनात्, 4.1.14 — the file carries it;
this rule does not use it; तद्धिताः — the heading begins here) — "[From here to the end of Book 5,
the affixes are called] taddhita." Three questions name the senses: "-ness?" D — **5.1.119 तस्य
भावस्त्वतलौ** [D] — "In the sense 'the being of X', त्व and तल्"; "has?" D — **5.2.94
तदस्यास्त्यस्मिन्निति मतुप्** [D] — "In the sense 'X is his' or 'X is in it', मतुप्" (the Kāśikā's
गोमान् and वृक्षवान्, K27); "descendant of?" D — **4.1.92 तस्यापत्यम्** [D] — "In the sense 'X's
offspring'". The learner answers the question, not the number.

**Step 2 — the table (§15 (1)).** -ता/-त्व (✓): एकता, ममता, पात्रता, गुरुत्वम्, बालत्वम्, पात्रत्वम्; the -ता
is feminine and takes the feminine -आ, D — **4.1.4 अजाद्यतष्टाप्** [D] — "After a base in अ, in the
feminine, टाप्." -मत्/-वत् (✓ nominatives): श्रीमान्, धीमान्, धनवान्, बलवान्, गुणवान्; D — **8.2.9
मादुपधायाश्च मतोर्वोऽयवादिभ्यः** [D] — "After a base ending in म् or अ/आ, or with म् or अ/आ before its last
sound, the म् of मतुप् becomes व् — not after यव and the rest." -इन् (✓ nom. sg.): पक्षी, बली, सुखी, धनी;
D — **5.2.115 अत इनिठनौ** [D] — "After a base in अ, in the same sense, इनि or ठन्" (and मतुप् too, the
Kāśikā adds: दण्डवान् beside दण्डी, K35; the Kāśikā's own बलवान्, बली stand under 5.2.136, and its सुखी under 5.2.131, Teacher Notes).

**Step 3 — vṛddhi, and the bha base.** Q — **7.2.117 तद्धितेष्वचामादेः** [Q]. Padaccheda (data file):
तद्धितेषु (सप्तमी, plural) · अचाम् (षष्ठी, plural) · आदेः (षष्ठी, singular). The locative is the context
"before taddhitas" (1.1.66 [Q, owned]: the operation is on what precedes); आदेः is the genitive "in
place of" (1.1.49 [Q, owned]) — "in place of the first"; अचाम् is a genitive of the group, "of the
vowels" — the Kāśikā: *aṅgasya acām ādeḥ acaḥ sthāne* "in place of the first vowel among the vowels
of the aṅga" (K23). Bracket line (0.H): [carried (data file): वृद्धिः, 7.2.114; अचः, 7.2.115; ञ्णिति,
7.2.115] — the ñ/ṇ condition is carried. English: "Before a ñ- or ṇ-marked taddhita, the first vowel
of the aṅga is replaced by vṛddhi." D — **6.4.148 यस्येति च** [D] — "Of a bha base ending in इ/ई or
अ/आ, the final vowel is dropped before ई and before a taddhita"; the base is bha only before a
taddhita beginning with a vowel or य् (1.4.18 [Q, owned]) — so not before -ता, -त्व, -मत्: एकता keeps its
अ. With the teacher, side by side: F-i (पाणिनीयम्: D — **1.1.73
वृद्धिर्यस्याचामादिस्तद् वृद्धम्** [D] — "A word whose first vowel is a vṛddhi vowel is called vṛddha";
D — **4.2.114 वृद्धाच्छः** [D] — "After a vṛddha base, छ"; D — **7.1.2 आयनेयीनीयियः फढखच्छघां
प्रत्ययादीनाम्** [D] — "At an affix's beginning, फ, ढ, ख, छ, घ are replaced by आयन्, एय्, ईन्, ईय्, इय्") and
F-j (लौकिकम्: D — **5.1.44 लोकसर्वलोकाट्ठञ्** [D] — "After लोक and सर्वलोक, in the sense 'known in it',
ठञ्"; D — **7.3.50 ठस्येकः** [D] — "The ठ of an affix is replaced by इक"). The marked line in both:
the ending of the phrase goes (lesson 4: 2.4.71).

**Step 4 — a descendant, shown once (○).** D — **4.1.95 अत इञ्** [D] — "After a base in अ, in the sense
'offspring', इञ्." F-m, with the teacher: दशरथ → दाशरथिः, Rāma. Display only: no item asks it.

**Step 5 — the card (§15 (2)).** -तर/-तम (✓ as forms; 5.3.57, 5.3.55 [K]): प्रियतर, प्रियतम,
बलवत्तर; यावत् … तावत् (✓ the pair; 5.2.39 [K]): "as long as … so long" — the pair Volume 3 left to this
chapter (v3.c05.l03); -मय (◐; 4.3.143 [K]): अमृतमय "made of nectar"; -वत् "like" (◐; 5.1.115 [K]): पुत्रवत्,
and the Hitopadeśa's आत्मवत् (Hit 1.14, the card line), "as towards oneself", whose comparison stands in the locative
(5.1.116, Teacher Notes); -मात्र (◐; Teacher Notes); -तः (◐; the four
owned); -क (◐; बालकः). Each is read off the card; none of its rules is asked.

### §9–§11 Pronunciation, IPA, mouth

Drill: एकता /eːkɐt̪ɑː/ · ममता /mɐmɐt̪ɑː/ · गुरुत्वम् /guɾut̪ʋɐm/ · बलवान् /bɐlɐʋɑːn̪/ · श्रीमान् /ɕɾiːmɑːn̪/ ·
पक्षी /pɐkʂiː/ · लौकिक /lɐu̯kikɐ/ · पाणिनीयम् /pɑːɳin̪iːjɐm/ · दैवम् /d̪ɐi̯ʋɐm/ · व्याकरणम् /ʋjɑːkɐɾɐɳɐm/ ·
बलवत्तरः /bɐlɐʋɐt̪t̪ɐɾɐh/. Mouth: the vṛddhi diphthongs — औ of लौकिक opens on अ and closes to उ; ऐ of दैवम्
on अ to इ: one syllable each, never two.

### §12 Common mistakes (six rows)

| Mistake | Fix | Why |
|---|---|---|
| \*ऐकता | एकता | तल् has no ञ् or ण्: no vṛddhi (7.2.117) |
| \*एक्ता | एकता | before -ता the base is pada, not bha: nothing drops (6.4.148 needs bha) |
| \*बलमान् | बलवान् | after a base in अ, the म् of मतुप् → व् (8.2.9) |
| \*लोकिक | लौकिक | ठञ् is ñ-marked: vṛddhi (7.2.117) |
| \*पाणिनियम् | पाणिनीयम् | छ → ईय (7.1.2), long ई |
| \*यदा गुरुर्वदति, तावत् … | यावद्गुरुर्वदति, तावत् … | the pair is यावत् … तावत् |


### §13 Examples — model sentences

1. **पाणिनेरिदं पाणिनीयम्।** — pause: पाणिनेः इदम् पाणिनीयम्। — *pāṇiner idaṃ pāṇinīyam.* — "This, of
   Pāṇini, is the Pāṇinīya."
2. **बलमस्यास्तीति बली।** — pause: बलम् अस्य अस्ति इति बली। — *balam asyāstīti balī.* — "'He has
   strength' — so, बली."
3. **सिंहो मूषकाद्बलवत्तरः।** — pause: सिंहः मूषकात् बलवत्तरः। — *siṃho mūṣakād balavattaraḥ.* — "The lion
   is stronger than the mouse."
4. **अयं पण्डितो व्याकरणशास्त्रं जानाति।** — pause: अयम् पण्डितः व्याकरण-शास्त्रम् जानाति। — *ayaṃ paṇḍito
   vyākaraṇaśāstraṃ jānāti.* — "This scholar knows the science of grammar."
5. **यावद्गुरुर्वदति, तावच्छात्राः शृण्वन्ति।** — pause: यावत् गुरुः वदति, तावत् छात्राः शृण्वन्ति। — *yāvad
   gurur vadati, tāvac chātrāḥ śṛṇvanti.* — "As long as the teacher speaks, the students listen."
   (The plan's शिष्याः re-worded to छात्राः: तावत् + शि- is the ◐ seam त् + श; त् + छ → च्छ is owned; §7,
   deviation 5.)
6. **देवस्येदं दैवम्।** — pause: देवस्य इदम् दैवम्। — *devasyedaṃ daivam.* — "What belongs to a god is दैवम्,
   fate."

Below the sentences, the headword's source line (Hit 0.31, table 0.J): **पूर्वजन्मकृतं कर्म तद्दैवमिति
कथ्यते।** — "The deed done in a former birth: that is called fate." (कथ्यते glossed; जन्म glossed
"birth", l04's headword.) Source: "Hitopadeśa, Introduction, 0.31; text of the GRETIL e-text
[REF:hitopadesha 0.31]".

Seams: एः → एर् before a vowel (पाणिनेरिदम्); अ + अ → आ, इ + इ → ई; त् → द् before a voiced sound; त् + छ
→ च्छ; उः → उर् (गुरुर्वदति); ओ for अः.

### §15 Grammar — the cards

1. **The taddhita table** (table 0.C, first part), ✓/◐/○ as printed there, with पात्रत्वम् and धनी
   (Monier-Williams s.v. pātra-tva, "worthiness … Hit."; Whitney §1230 *dhanín*).
2. **The card** (table 0.C, second part), with Hit 1.14 as its -वत् line (table 0.J), read, not asked.
3. **The marker box:** ञ्, ण् → vṛddhi (7.2.117); none → no change; a k-marked taddhita does the same
   (Teacher Notes); the -क of बालकः is no k-marker (1.3.8).
4. **Bha or pada before a taddhita:** a vowel or य् first → bha (1.4.18): पाणिन्-ईय, लौक्-इक; a
   consonant first → pada (1.4.17 [Q, owned]): एक-ता, बल-वत्.
5. **Paradigm ledger at exit of l03:**

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| -ता/-त्व words (every cell) | ✓ |
| -मत्/-वत्, -इन् words (nominative, as row 0.10 and the -इन् nom. sg.) | ✓ |
| their other cells | ◐ (c05.l03) |
| -तर/-तम; यावत् … तावत् | ✓ |
| -इक, -ईय words (लौकिक, पाणिनीय): derived, not coined | ◐ (the pattern) |
| -मय, -वत् "like", -मात्र, -तः, -क | ◐ |
| -इ patronymics (दाशरथिः) | ○ |
| the -ईयस्/-इष्ठ comparatives (श्रेष्ठ, owned, shown in Teacher Notes) | ○ |

### §16 Conversation (ten turns; homework)

Two students, सीता and गोपालः.

1. सीता — **त्वं किं पठसि?** — pause: त्वम् किम् पठसि? — *tvaṃ kiṃ paṭhasi?* — "What do you study?"
2. गोपालः — **अहं व्याकरणं पठामि।** — pause: अहम् व्याकरणम् पठामि। — *ahaṃ vyākaraṇaṃ paṭhāmi.* — "I study
   grammar."
3. सीता — **किं पाणिनीयं व्याकरणम्?** — pause: किम् पाणिनीयम् व्याकरणम्? — *kiṃ pāṇinīyaṃ vyākaraṇam?* —
   "Pāṇini's grammar?"
4. गोपालः — **आम्, पाणिनीयम्। मम गुरुः पण्डितः।** — pause: आम्, पाणिनीयम्। मम गुरुः पण्डितः। — *ām,
   pāṇinīyam. mama guruḥ paṇḍitaḥ.* — "Yes, Pāṇini's. My teacher is a scholar."
5. सीता — **तव गुरुर्वेदमपि जानाति?** — pause: तव गुरुः वेदम् अपि जानाति? — *tava gurur vedam api
   jānāti?* — "Does your teacher know the Veda too?"
6. गोपालः — **आम्। स वेदं व्याकरणं च जानाति।** — pause: आम्। सः वेदम् व्याकरणम् च जानाति। — *ām. sa
   vedaṃ vyākaraṇaṃ ca jānāti.* — "Yes. He knows the Veda and grammar."
7. सीता — **व्याकरणं तव प्रियम्?** — pause: व्याकरणम् तव प्रियम्? — *vyākaraṇaṃ tava priyam?* — "Is
   grammar dear to you?"
8. गोपालः — **आम्। व्याकरणं मम प्रियतमं शास्त्रम्।** — pause: आम्। व्याकरणम् मम प्रियतमम्
   शास्त्रम्। — *ām. vyākaraṇaṃ mama priyatamaṃ śāstram.* — "Yes. Grammar is the science dearest
   to me."
9. सीता — **अहमपि व्याकरणं ज्ञातुमिच्छामि।** — pause: अहम् अपि व्याकरणम् ज्ञातुम् इच्छामि। — *aham api
   vyākaraṇaṃ jñātum icchāmi.* — "I too want to know grammar."
10. गोपालः — **यावत्त्वं पठसि, तावदहं साहाय्यं करिष्यामि।** — pause: यावत् त्वम् पठसि, तावत् अहम्
    साहाय्यम् करिष्यामि। — *yāvat tvaṃ paṭhasi, tāvad ahaṃ sāhāyyaṃ kariṣyāmi.* — "As long as you
    study, I will help."

ज्ञातुम्: on l02's kṛt card (table 0.A's -तुम् column; Apte's root entry of ज्ञा, *jñātuṃ*).
Seams: उः → उर् before a voiced sound; m + vowel joined; त् + त; त् → द् before a vowel.

### §17 Story — the serial, episode 3

1. **मकरो वानरं नयन्समुद्रं प्रविशति।** — pause: मकरः वानरम् नयन् समुद्रम् प्रविशति। — *makaro vānaraṃ
   nayan samudraṃ praviśati.* — "Taking the monkey with him, the crocodile enters the sea."
2. **वानरः सुखी हसति। मकरः शनैश्चिन्तयति।** — pause: वानरः सुखी हसति। मकरः शनैः चिन्तयति। — *vānaraḥ
   sukhī hasati. makaraḥ śanaiś cintayati.* — "The monkey laughs, happy. The crocodile thinks, slowly."
3. **मम भार्ययोक्तम् — वानरस्य हृदयममृतमयम्।** — pause: मम भार्यया उक्तम् — वानरस्य हृदयम् अमृत-मयम्। —
   *mama bhāryayoktam — vānarasya hṛdayam amṛtamayam.* — "'My wife said: the monkey's heart is made of
   nectar.'"
4. **सोऽमृतमयानि फलानि खादति, अतस्तस्य हृदयममृतमयम्।** — pause: सः अमृत-मयानि फलानि खादति, अतः तस्य
   हृदयम् अमृत-मयम्। — *so 'mṛtamayāni phalāni khādati, atas tasya hṛdayam amṛtamayam.* — "'He eats
   fruits made of nectar; so his heart is made of nectar.'"
5. **मकरो वानरं पश्यन्न किमपि वदति।** — pause: मकरः वानरम् पश्यन् न किम् अपि वदति। — *makaro vānaraṃ
   paśyan na kim api vadati.* — "Looking at the monkey, the crocodile says nothing."
6. **वानरः पृच्छति — मित्र, किं चिन्तयसि?** — pause: वानरः पृच्छति — मित्र, किम् चिन्तयसि? — *vānaraḥ
   pṛcchati — mitra, kiṃ cintayasi?* — "The monkey asks: 'Friend, what are you thinking?'"

Questions: (a) What did the wife say of the monkey's heart? (it is made of nectar) (b) Why? (he eats
fruits made of nectar). अमृतमय (◐) is read with the card; its source is c03.l05's N2, *amṛta-mayaṃ*
(`vispancu.htm` line 5228). Seams: सोऽमृत- (अः + अ → ओ, the अ marked by avagraha, as सोऽब्रवीत् in N3a);
शनैश्चिन्तयति (visarga → श् before च); भार्ययोक्तम् (आ + उ → ओ); पश्यन्न (न् before न, written together);
m + vowel joined; visarga → स् before त (अतस्तस्य).

### §18 Exercises

**Ex 1 (objective 1; 8 items).** Make the word, nominative singular, and give its meaning: (1) the
-ता word of मम (2) the -ता word of पात्र (3) the -त्व word of पात्र (4) the -वत् word of गुण (5) the -मत्
word of धी ["thought, intellect"] (6) the -इन् word of पक्ष (7) the -इन् word of सुख (8) the -इन् word of धन.
*Key:* (1) ममता "mine-ness, selfishness" (2) पात्रता "worthiness" (3) पात्रत्वम् "worthiness" (4) गुणवान्
"having good qualities" (5) धीमान् "wise" (6) पक्षी "winged; a bird" (7) सुखी "happy" (8) धनी "rich".

**Ex 2 (objective 2; 12 items).** Sort into two boxes — A: the first vowel took vṛddhi; B: it did
not — and for each word in A say which vowel became which; for each in B say why nothing happened:
एकता, लौकिकम्, गुरुत्वम्, धनवान्, दैवम्, पक्षी, बालत्वम्, पाणिनीयम्, ममता, श्रीमान्, सुखी, पात्रता.
*Key:* A: लौकिकम् (लो- → लौ-: ओ → औ), दैवम् (दे- → दै-: ए → ऐ; Whitney §1208h, *dāíva* from *devá*; the affix
of दैवम् is not asked, its row being ◐). B: एकता, गुरुत्वम्, बालत्वम्, ममता, पात्रता
(तल्/त्व: no ञ् or ण्), धनवान्, श्रीमान् (मतुप्: none), पक्षी, सुखी (इनि: none), पाणिनीयम् (छ: none; its आ was
already there — that is why पाणिनि is vṛddha). One point per word.

**Ex 3 (objective 3; 6 items; derive-it-yourself).** (1) Derive, according to Pāṇini, एक + तल् → एकता
in table form. (2) Derive, according to Pāṇini, बल + मतुप् → बलवान्. (3)–(6) Answer according to Pāṇini. (3) Why does पाणिनि take छ? (4) What replaces the ठ of
ठञ्? (5) Why does the इ of पाणिनि drop before ईय, while the अ of एक stays before -ता? (6) In बलवान्, where
does the व् come from?
*Key:* (1) F-k (2) F-l (3) its first vowel, आ, is a vṛddhi vowel: it is vṛddha (1.1.73), and after a
vṛddha base comes छ (4.2.114) (4) इक (7.3.50) (5) before a vowel-initial taddhita the base is bha and
its final इ drops (6.4.148); before -ता, consonant-initial, it is pada (6) after a base in अ, the म् of
मतुप् becomes व् (8.2.9). D-tier rules by number or gist.

**Ex 4 (objective 4; 6 items).** Complete: (1) व्याघ्रः पान्थात्___। [बलवत्, "stronger"] (2) इदं मम ___
मित्रम्। [प्रिय, "dearest"] (3) सीता ___ बालिका। [प्रिय, "dearer"] (4) ___ गुरुर्वदति, तावच्छात्रा लिखन्ति।
(5) यावत् सः पठति, ___ अहम् लिखामि। [pause form] (6) मम ___ मित्रं त्वम्। [प्रिय, "dearer"]
*Key:* (1) बलवत्तरः (2) प्रियतमम् (3) प्रियतरा (4) यावत् (5) तावत् (6) प्रियतरम्; the joined shapes before the next
word — प्रियतमं and प्रियतरं before मित्रम्, यावद् before गुरुः — score the same.

### §19 Quiz (correct option first)

1. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, a taddhita is added after — **a nominal base** · a root · a verb
   ending
2. [FACT] "Oneness": — **एकता** · एकदा · एकः
3. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini (7.2.117), the first vowel takes vṛddhi before — **a ñ- or ṇ-marked
   taddhita** · every taddhita · every kṛt
4. [FACT] बलमस्यास्तीति ___। — **बली** · बालकः · बलम्
5. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, पाणिनि takes छ because — **its first vowel is a vṛddhi vowel: it is
   vṛddha** · it ends in इ · it is a name
6. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, the इ of पाणिनि drops in पाणिनीयम् because — **before a
   vowel-initial taddhita the base is bha, and its final इ drops** · the affix gives vṛddhi · the affix
   is k-marked
7. [FACT] "Stronger (of the two)": — **बलवत्तरः** · बलवान् · बली
8. [FACT] ___ गुरुर्वदति, तावच्छात्राः शृण्वन्ति। — **यावत्** · यदा · ततः
9. [FACT] दैवम् means — **fate, destiny** · a god · the Veda
10. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, the व् of बलवान् is — **the म् of मतुप्, after a base in अ** · the
    first sound of a second word · the last sound of बल

### §20 Revision — the bookings

- +1 day: six new words — make, in the nominative singular, the -ता word of एक, the -त्व word of गुरु
  and of बाल, the -वत् word of धन, the -मत् word of श्री, and the -इन् word of बल; v4.c04.l04's Station 0
  runs exactly this.
- +1 week: derive पाणिनीयम् — derive, according to Pāṇini, in table form, पाणिनि + छ → पाणिनीयम्, each
  step with its sūtra; v4.c04.l05's Station 0 runs exactly this.

### §21 Homework (47 minutes)

1. (4) Ex 1 items 5–8. 2. (12) Ex 3: items 1–2 (five each), 3–6 (two). 3. (5) Ex 4. 4. (6) §19 quiz,
closed-book, then its key. 5. (5) §16 aloud. 6. (5) §17 aloud, then its two questions. 7. (3) The
Hit 1.14 card line aloud, with its glosses. 8. (3) The §9 drill. 9. (4) The vocabulary card (six
headwords, a sentence each). Sum 4 + 12 + 5 + 6 + 5 + 5 + 3 + 3 + 4 = 47.

### §22 Teacher Notes

**Timing plan (45):** 0–10 Station 0 (item 1 three, item 2 six, prompts one) → 10–12 §1–§2 → 12–18 Steps
1–2 (4.1.76; the three questions; the table) → 18–24 Step 3 (7.2.117's padaccheda; 6.4.148 and bha;
F-i and F-j side by side) → 24–27 Step 4 (F-m, display) → 27–31 Step 5 (the card) → 31–35 §13 1–6 →
35–40 Ex 2, in pairs → 40–43 Ex 1 items 1–4 → 43–45 §20, homework. Sum 10 + 2 + 6 + 6 + 3 + 4 + 4 + 5 +
3 + 2 = 45. Homework: Ex 1 (5)–(8), Ex 3, Ex 4, §19, §16, §17, the card line, §9.

**Teacher-Notes rules:** 7.2.118 किति च (a k-marked taddhita gives vṛddhi too) · 4.3.101 तेन प्रोक्तम्
(पाणिनीयम् "taught by"; K38) · 4.3.120 तस्येदम् (दैवम्, पाणिनेरिदम्; K39), with the vārttika देवाद्यञञौ under 4.1.85 (the Kāśikā, lines 27947–27949: दैवम्'s affix is अञ्, not अण्) · 5.2.37 प्रमाणे
द्वयसज्दघ्नञ्मात्रचः (-मात्र) · 5.1.116 तत्र तस्येव (Hit 1.14's मातृवत्, लोष्ट्रवत्, आत्मवत्: "as towards a mother", the standard in the locative,
as in the Kāśikā's *mathurāyām iva mathurāvat*) · 5.3.7 पञ्चम्यास्तसिल् (-तः) · 5.3.70 प्रागिवात्कः and 5.4.29 यावादिभ्यः कन्
(-क) · 4.2.59 तदधीते तद्वेद and 7.3.3 न य्वाभ्यां पदान्ताभ्याम् पूर्वौ तु ताभ्यामैच् (वैयाकरणः, ○) · 4.1.82
समर्थानां प्रथमाद्वा (the affix after the first word of the phrase) · 5.2.136 बलादिभ्यो मतुबन्यतरस्याम्
(बलवान्, बली: the Kāśikā's own pair) and 5.2.131 सुखादिभ्यश्च (सुखी: इनि only) · 6.1.68 (सुँ gone after the feminine
-आ, F-k's last line) · the ट् of टाप् a marker (1.3.7, Teacher Notes since c02). **Card rules [K]:**
5.3.57, 5.3.55, 4.3.143, 5.1.115, 5.2.39 with K40–K44. **Commentary lines:** K23–K39. The comparative
श्रेष्ठ (owned) is -इष्ठ, 5.3.60 ff., shown here and not in learner text.

**Payoff.** लौकिक, एकता, ममता (Volume 1), पक्षी, सुखी, श्रीमान्, धीमान् (Volumes 2–3) are derived or
derivable; पात्रता, पात्रत्व (v3.c06.l04) are made by 5.1.119; यावत् … तावत् (v3.c05.l03) is taught; the
-तः of v1.c02.l06 has its suffix story; पाणिनीयम्, the volume's name, derives itself.

**Previews and deferrals.** The cells of -मत्/-वत्/-इन् beyond the nominative: c05.l03. The apatya system
beyond one display, the -ईयस्/-इष्ठ comparatives, the gender suffixes beyond -आ/-ई: Volume 5 reading.

**Verified pointers.** v3.c06.l04 lines 340–344 (पात्रताम्, "the formation is v4.c04's") and 611;
v3.c05.l03 lines 567, 660 and 1135 (यावत् … तावत्; -वत्/-मात्र to v4.c04); v1.c02.l06 (the -तः words, the
ledger); v4.c02.l02 (vṛddhi as substitute; 1.1.1); v4.c03.l04 (7.2.116, the ṇ of णल्).

**IR-005 (1.x pp. 54, 79–80).** The 1.x book taught these suffixes twice; this lesson is the single strand. Quoted
from the page images and corrected here: p. 79 files दाशरथिः under अण् — it is इञ्, by 4.1.95, as F-m derives it — and
वैयाकरणः under ठक्/ठञ्, though its rules are 4.2.59 and 7.3.3, with no ठ and no -इक; it glosses मतुप् and वतुप् alike as
"having", though वतुप् is 5.2.39's, the affix of यावत्; and p. 54 gives one -वत् for "having, like", where "having" is
मतुप्, 5.2.94, and "like" is वति, 5.1.115.

**Main risk:** vṛddhi on every taddhita (\*ऐकता). Fix: the marker box (§15 (3)) and Ex 2.

### §23–§26 Assets

Image as the media line. Video (45 s): एक + ता sliding together with no change; लोक + इक with ओ lighting
to औ. Voice-over: §5. Reel: "\*ऐकता? — एकता. No ञ्, ण् or क् marker, no vṛddhi." End card: → v4.c04.l04.

## 4. L4 — समासः: Compounds as Derivations (grammar)

**Front matter.** id `v4.c04.l04` · file `lesson-04-compounds-as-derivations.md` · title sa समासः · sa_iast
*samāsaḥ* · en "Compounds as Derivations" · hi "समास: व्युत्पत्ति के रूप में" · lesson_type grammar · cefr
B1 · duration 45 · self_study_minutes **46** · prerequisites [v4.c04.l03] · review_of [v4.c04.l03,
v4.c04.l02, v4.c02.l01, v3.c05.l04, v1.c03.l02] · vocabulary_count **5** · tags [grammar, panini,
samasa, compound, tatpurusha, upapada, samasanta, prati-padika, luk, accent-card, mahabhashya,
serial, spaced-review] · media (planned): image `v4c04l04-compound-steps.svg` (two padas with their
endings, a bracket closing round them, the inner ending struck out, the न् of राजन् fading; a small
ड beside जन् eating its अन्); audio `v4c04l04-derivation-read.mp3`, `v4c04l04-mahabhashya-read.mp3`,
`v4c04l04-conversation.mp3`; worksheet `v4c04l04-compounds.pdf`; quizzes `v4c04l04-quiz.md`,
`v4c04l04-quiz-key.md`.

**Objectives:**
1. en "Build the genitive tatpuruṣa of two owned nouns from its vigraha — with the न् of an n-stem
   first member dropped — and the compounds in -राज and -सख." · hi "विग्रह से दो परिचित संज्ञाओं का षष्ठी-तत्पुरुष
   बनाएँ — नकारान्त पहले पद का न् हटाकर — और -राज तथा -सख वाले समास भी।" (Ex 1, 8 items)
2. en "Make the upapada compound in -ज, 'born in X', from an owned noun in the locative." · hi "सप्तमी
   में किसी परिचित संज्ञा से -ज वाला उपपद-समास, 'X में जन्मा', बनाएँ।" (Ex 2, 6 items)
3. en "Derive, according to Pāṇini, राजपुरुषः, जलजम् and कपोतराजः with the teacher, and जन्मदिनम्,
   पङ्कजम् and महाराजः by yourself." · hi "पाणिनि के अनुसार शिक्षक के साथ राजपुरुषः, जलजम् और कपोतराजः की,
   और स्वयं जन्मदिनम्, पङ्कजम् और महाराजः की व्युत्पत्ति करें।" (Ex 3, 6 items)
4. en "Give the vigraha of compounds of the four kinds and name the kind, and give the two readings of
   इन्द्रशत्रुः with their meanings." · hi "चारों प्रकार के समासों का विग्रह देकर प्रकार बताएँ, और
   इन्द्रशत्रुः के दोनों अर्थ विग्रह सहित दें।" (Ex 4, 8 items)

**The opening (§1).** जलज "water-born; lotus" has been owned since v1.c03.l02 (खग "bird", owned since v1.c03.l01, is an upapada compound too). Volume 3
built compounds from their vigraha by four kinds and glossed कपोतराजः with a pointer: -राज at a
compound's end is v4.c04's (v3.c05.l04 §8 "What waits"). Question: if राज्ञः पुरुषः gives राजपुरुषः, why
does कपोतानां राजा give कपोतराजः and not \*कपोतराजा?

**The one grammar point.** A compound is a derivation: two padas combine (2.1.3–4), the whole is a
nominal base (1.2.46), its inner endings are deleted (2.4.71), and a first member that is a pada keeps
the pada's rules (8.2.7: राजन् → राज-). Some compounds take a final affix of their own (5.4.91); in
the upapada kind the kṛt affix and the compound are made together (3.2.97, 2.2.19).

### §3 Station 0 (9 minutes)

> **Station 0 — स्मरणम्**
> - +1 day: six new words — make, in the nominative singular, the -ता word of एक, the -त्व word of गुरु
>   and of बाल, the -वत् word of धन, the -मत् word of श्री, and the -इन् word of बल; v4.c04.l04's Station 0
>   runs exactly this. **(3 min)**
> - +1 week: derive आगत्य — derive, according to Pāṇini, in table form, आ + गम् + क्त्वा → आगत्य, each
>   step with its sūtra; v4.c04.l04's Station 0 runs exactly this. **(5 min: 4 to write, 1 to mark)**
> - Prompts (unscored, 1 min): what does 8.2.7 drop? (v4.c02.l01) · the vigraha of विद्यालयः
>   (v3.c05.l04) · जलज, खग — their meanings? (v1.c03.l02)

Key (✓ only): S0-fe, S0-fd as table 0.I (in S0-fd the compound line is a marked step: "आ + गम् make a
compound — today's rule"); prompts: the न् at the end of a nominal base that is a pada (8.2.7, a
D rule, by gist) · विद्यायाः आलयः · water-born, lotus; sky-goer, bird. §3 reaches every lesson of
review_of.

### §4 Callouts

- **TRADITION.** According to Pāṇini, the rules from 2.1.3 up to the one before 2.2.38 (कडाराः कर्मधारये) are about samāsa; a pada with a
  case ending compounds with another (2.1.4), and the endings inside the compound are deleted (2.4.71
  — the Kāśikā's own example is राजपुरुषः). [REF:ashtadhyayi 2.1.3]; [REF:kashika 2.4.71]
- **TRADITION.** The verse the Mahābhāṣya quotes on इन्द्रशत्रुः (M1, printed in full: table 0.E's box). Source
  line: "Mahābhāṣya, Paspaśāhnika; text of the GRETIL e-text (Kielhorn's edition)"
  [REF:mahabhashya Paspaśāhnika].
- **FACT.** Whitney: compounds whose last member is an ordinary noun "are regularly and usually
  accented on the final syllable" (§1267); the possessive (bahuvrīhi) "oftenest … has as a compound
  the natural accent of its prior member" (§1295). [REF:whitney1889 §1267]; §1295

### §5 What the lesson corrects (plain text)

IR-026 (X1): the 1.x book glosses the two readings of इन्द्रशत्रु as "May Indra's slayer prosper"
(tatpuruṣa) and "May the slayer of Indra prosper" (bahuvrīhi) — one meaning twice. The tatpuruṣa is
इन्द्रस्य शत्रुः "Indra's enemy (his slayer)"; the bahuvrīhi is इन्द्रः शत्रुः यस्य सः "he whose enemy is
Indra" — as v3.c05.l04's Teacher Notes already said. The story behind the word (Tvaṣṭṛ's sacrifice) is not told: no locus for it is cited in
REFERENCES.md (plan Q5).

### §6–§7 Sanskrit and Hindi explanation

§6, two lines:
1. **राज्ञः पुरुषो राजपुरुषः। पदद्वयमेकः शब्दो भवति।** — pause: राज्ञः पुरुषः राजपुरुषः। पद-द्वयम् एकः
   शब्दः भवति। — "The king's man: राजपुरुषः. Two padas become one word." (द्वयम्, c03.l04's headword;
   पदम्, शब्दः owned. Two first drafts were set aside: one used the dual द्वे पदे and the passive
   लुप्यते, neither owned.)
2. **जले जायत इति जलजम्।** — model sentence 2, read in §6 as the upapada's sense.
§7 (Hindi): Hindi's own compounds of this shape (राजपुरुष, जन्मदिन) are the course's glosses only, not
a FACT callout (row 0.17).

### §8 English explanation — the steps

**Step 1 — the heading and the two padas.** D — **2.1.3 प्राक् कडारात् समासः** [D] (heading begins
here) — "[The rules] before कडार (2.2.38) are about samāsa." D — **2.1.4 सह सुपा** [D] (heading begins
here) — "A word with a case ending compounds with a word with a case ending." Which pair, and of which
kind, is said by the rules that follow — for the genitive kind, a rule in Teacher Notes; the name
tatpuruṣa is 2.1.22 [K], read off the card.

**Step 2 — one word, its endings gone, its first member still a pada.** D — **1.2.46
कृत्तद्धितसमासाश्च** [D] (bracket: प्रातिपदिकम्, 1.2.45) — "A word ending in a kṛt or a taddhita, and a
compound, are also called prātipadika" — the rule the marked lines of l01–l03 pointed to. D — **2.4.71
सुपो धातुप्रातिपदिकयोः** [D] (bracket: लुक्, 2.4.58) — "A case ending inside a root or a nominal base is
deleted." With the teacher: F-n (राजपुरुषः), with 8.2.7 [D, owned] for the न् of राजन् — still a pada
though its ending is gone (a labelled step; the rule is in Teacher Notes) — and the vigraha's राज्ञः
derived on the spot from owned tools, as v4.c02.l01 derived राज्ञा. The other cells of राजन्, जन्मन् and
मन्त्रिन् are c05.l03's.

**Step 3 — born in the water: upapada.** D — **3.2.97 सप्तम्यां जनेर्डः** [D] (bracket: भूते, 3.2.84, by
name) — "After जन्, with a word in the locative as upapada, ड." (Under भूते the -ज word means "born in X": the Kāśikā paraphrases its example *upasare jātaḥ*, with the -त; the vigraha with जायते is the school form the course keeps.) D — **6.4.143 टेः** [D] — "Before a
ḍ-marked affix, the ṭi drops"; D — **1.1.64 अचोऽन्त्यादि टि** [D] — "The last vowel, with what follows
it, is called ṭi": in जन्, अन्. The rule stands under the heading भस्य, which the volume does not teach
(the bracket's "further headings: Teacher Notes"), and जन् before a kṛt is not bha; the Kāśikā applies
it all the same, "since the marker ड् would otherwise be there for nothing" (K53). It is the rule of
the ḍ-marker on c03.l03's anubandha card, the one that gives भविता from डा (c03.l05's card). D —
**2.2.19 उपपदमतिङ्** [D] — "An upapada that is not a tiṅ-final word always compounds with the word it
goes with (the kṛt form): a tatpuruṣa" — and, the Kāśikā says, before that word has an ending of its own (K51). With the teacher:
F-o (जलजम्).

**Step 4 — a final affix of the compound's own.** D — **5.4.91 राजाहस्सखिभ्यष्टच्** [D] (bracket:
तत्पुरुषस्य, 5.4.86) — "After a tatpuruṣa ending in राजन्, अहन् or सखि, the samāsānta टच्" (the Kāśikā:
महाराजः, राजसखः). Then, before the affix's अ, the अन् of राजन् goes (Teacher Notes): कपोतराजन् + अ → कपोतराज् + अ
→ **कपोतराजः**, an a-stem (F-p, with the teacher). D — **6.3.46 आन्महतः समानाधिकरणजातीययोः** [D] —
"Before a second member in the same case, महत् → महा": महाराजः (the learner's F-q). Not before a
genitive: the Kāśikā's महत्पुत्रः "a great man's son" (K55).

**Step 5 — the kinds, filed as Pāṇini files them.** Table 0.D: tatpuruṣa, with karmadhāraya and dvigu
inside it; bahuvrīhi (2.2.24, owned); dvandva (2.2.29, owned; मातापितरौ, its माता-: Teacher Notes);
avyayībhāva (◐); and the group of 2.2.18 — D — **2.2.18 कुगतिप्रादयः** [D] — "कु, the gati words and प्र
and the rest always compound with a word able to join them; the compound is a tatpuruṣa": सुप्रभातम्
(owned since v2.c01), प्रभातम्; the gati compounds ऊरीकृत्य, स्वीकृत्य ◐ (glossed). This is also the rule
that made आ + गम् a compound in l02's आगत्य.

**Step 6 — the accent card (IR-026).** Table 0.D's accent card: TRADITION (6.1.223, 6.2.1 [K]; the
Mahābhāṣya's M1 read aloud, and M2's last sentence paraphrased) beside FACT (Whitney §1267, §1295).
Asked: only the meaning contrast — इन्द्रस्य शत्रुः against इन्द्रः शत्रुः यस्य सः. No accent mark is
printed or asked (accent: Volume 5).

### §9–§11 Pronunciation, IPA, mouth

Drill: राजपुरुषः /ɾɑːd͡ʑɐpuɾuʂɐh/ · जलजम् /d͡ʑɐlɐd͡ʑɐm/ · कपोतराजः /kɐpoːt̪ɐɾɑːd͡ʑɐh/ · महाराजः
/mɐɦɑːɾɑːd͡ʑɐh/ · जन्मदिनम् /d͡ʑɐn̪mɐd̪in̪ɐm/ · मन्त्री /mɐn̪t̪ɾiː/ · वार्ता /ʋɑːɾt̪ɑː/ · स्वभावः /sʋɐbʱɑːʋɐh/ ·
युद्धम् /jud̪d̪ʱɐm/ · इन्द्रशत्रुः /in̪d̪ɾɐɕɐt̪ɾuh/. Mouth: a compound is one word — one breath, no pause at
the seam (राज-पुरुषः said as one); in युद्धम् hold the द before the breathy ध.

### §12 Common mistakes (six rows)

| Mistake | Fix | Why |
|---|---|---|
| \*राजन्पुरुषः | राजपुरुषः | the first member is a pada: its final न् drops (8.2.7) |
| \*राज्ञःपुरुषः | राजपुरुषः | the inner ending is deleted (2.4.71) |
| \*कपोतराजा | कपोतराजः | -राजन् at a tatpuruṣa's end takes टच् (5.4.91): an a-stem |
| \*महत्राजः | महाराजः | महत् → महा before a member in the same case (6.3.46) |
| \*जलजन् | जलजम् | before ड, the ṭi अन् of जन् drops (6.4.143) |
| "compounds have no grammar" | every compound has steps | 2.1.3–4, 1.2.46, 2.4.71, 8.2.7 |

### §13 Examples — model sentences

1. **राज्ञः पुरुषो राजपुरुषः।** — pause: राज्ञः पुरुषः राजपुरुषः। — *rājñaḥ puruṣo rājapuruṣaḥ.* — "The
   king's man: a rājapuruṣa."
2. **जले जायत इति जलजम्।** — pause: जले जायते इति जलजम्। — *jale jāyata iti jalajam.* — "It is born in
   water: so, jalaja (a lotus)."
3. **कपोतानां राजा कपोतराजः।** — pause: कपोतानाम् राजा कपोतराजः। — *kapotānāṃ rājā kapotarājaḥ.* — "The
   king of the doves: the dove-king."
4. **प्रभाते मन्त्री नृपाय वार्तां कथयति।** — pause: प्रभाते मन्त्री नृपाय वार्ताम् कथयति। — *prabhāte mantrī
   nṛpāya vārtāṃ kathayati.* — "At dawn the minister tells the king the news." (नृपाय, not राज्ञे: the
   राजन् cells beyond Step 2's are c05.l03's.)
5. **मम जन्मदिनं श्वः।** — pause: मम जन्मदिनम् श्वः। — *mama janmadinaṃ śvaḥ.* — "My birthday is
   tomorrow."
6. **स्वस्य भावः स्वभावः।** — pause: स्वस्य भावः स्वभावः। — *svasya bhāvaḥ svabhāvaḥ.* — "One's own being:
   nature." (भावः glossed "being, state".)

Seams: ओ for अः before a voiced sound; ए + इ — जायते इति → जायत इति (ए before a vowel → अय्, its य्
dropped: the owned vowel map); ṃ. स्वस्य: the genitive of स्व, owned since v3.c05 (स्वः).

> राजगृहम्, मृगराजः, वानरराजः, जलतीरम्; Ex 2: समुद्रजम्, देशजः, गृहजः, नदीजः, अग्निजः, भूमिजः). Tools added: 11
> (2.1.3, 2.1.4, 2.4.71, 5.4.91, 1.2.46, 2.2.18, 2.2.19, 3.2.97, 6.4.143, 1.1.64, 6.3.46); toolbox 131. ✓
> covers only the cells this lesson's tables print and the cells owned since Volume 3: the genitive
> tatpuruṣa built from its vigraha (8.2.7 for an n-stem first member), the -ज upapada on an owned noun
> in the locative, the -राज and -सख compounds (5.4.91); the vigraha of the four kinds as v3.c05.l04 made them ✓; जन्म in

> वार्ता, स्वभावः and युद्धम् in every cell of their stem classes.

lexicalised -त of युध्) count once (rules 10 and 18); the compounds of owned members made in the exercises
count 0 (rule 10).

### §16 Conversation (ten turns; homework)

The king and his minister, at dawn.

1. नृपः — **अद्य का वार्ता?** — pause: अद्य का वार्ता? — *adya kā vārtā?* — "What is the news today?"
2. मन्त्री — **महाराज, श्वस्तव पुत्रस्य जन्मदिनम्।** — pause: महाराज, श्वः तव पुत्रस्य जन्मदिनम्। — *mahārāja,
   śvas tava putrasya janmadinam.* — "Great king, tomorrow is your son's birthday."
3. नृपः — **सत्यम्! तस्य जन्मदिने किं करिष्यामः?** — pause: सत्यम्! तस्य जन्मदिने किम् करिष्यामः? —
   *satyam! tasya janmadine kiṃ kariṣyāmaḥ?* — "True! What shall we do on his birthday?"
4. मन्त्री — **राजपुरुषा ग्रामे भोजनं दास्यन्ति।** — pause: राजपुरुषाः ग्रामे भोजनम् दास्यन्ति। —
   *rājapuruṣā grāme bhojanaṃ dāsyanti.* — "The king's men will give food in the village."
5. नृपः — **भद्रम्। युद्धस्य का वार्ता?** — pause: भद्रम्। युद्धस्य का वार्ता? — *bhadram. yuddhasya kā
   vārtā?* — "Good. What news of the war?"
6. मन्त्री — **युद्धं नास्ति, महाराज। देशे शान्तिरस्ति।** — pause: युद्धम् न अस्ति, महाराज। देशे शान्तिः अस्ति। —
   *yuddhaṃ nāsti, mahārāja. deśe śāntir asti.* — "There is no war, great king. There is peace in the
   land."
7. नृपः — **मम पुत्रः कुत्र?** — pause: मम पुत्रः कुत्र? — *mama putraḥ kutra?* — "Where is my son?"
8. मन्त्री — **स विद्यालये पठति। तस्य स्वभावो मधुरः।** — pause: सः विद्यालये पठति। तस्य स्वभावः मधुरः। —
   *sa vidyālaye paṭhati. tasya svabhāvo madhuraḥ.* — "He studies at school. His nature is gentle."
9. नृपः — **अहं तस्य जन्मदिने तस्मै पुस्तकं दास्यामि।** — pause: अहम् तस्य जन्मदिने तस्मै पुस्तकम्
   दास्यामि। — *ahaṃ tasya janmadine tasmai pustakaṃ dāsyāmi.* — "On his birthday I will give him a
   book."
10. मन्त्री — **भद्रम्, महाराज!** — pause: भद्रम्, महाराज! — *bhadram, mahārāja!* — "Good, great king!"

Seams: श्वस्तव (visarga → स् before त); राजपुरुषा ग्रामे (आः before a voiced sound drops its visarga);
नास्ति (अ + अ → आ); शान्तिरस्ति (इः → इर् before a vowel); स्वभावो मधुरः.

### §17 Story — the serial, episode 4 (the Pañcatantra frame, the open sea; the reveal is c05.l03's)

1. **मकरः समुद्रस्य मध्यं गतः।** — pause: मकरः समुद्रस्य मध्यम् गतः। — *makaraḥ samudrasya madhyaṃ
   gataḥ.* — "The crocodile has gone to the middle of the sea."
2. **वानरस्य हृदये भयम्।** — pause: वानरस्य हृदये भयम्। — *vānarasya hṛdaye bhayam.* — "In the monkey's
   heart, fear."
3. **तस्य नाम रक्तमुखः — रक्तं मुखं यस्य सः।** — pause: तस्य नाम रक्त-मुखः — रक्तम् मुखम् यस्य सः। —
   *tasya nāma raktamukhaḥ — raktaṃ mukhaṃ yasya saḥ.* — "His name is Raktamukha — he whose face is
   red." (रक्तम् glossed "red"; a bahuvrīhi name, ○ display — v3.c06.l03's रक्तमुखो नाम वानरः.)
4. **स पृच्छति — मित्र, किमर्थं शीघ्रं गच्छसि?** — pause: सः पृच्छति — मित्र, किमर्थम् शीघ्रम्
   गच्छसि? — *sa pṛcchati — mitra, kimarthaṃ śīghraṃ gacchasi?* — "He asks: 'Friend, why do you go
   so fast?'"
5. **मकरो न वदति। स चिन्तयति — इदानीं सत्यं वक्ष्यामि।** — pause: मकरः न वदति। सः चिन्तयति — इदानीम्
   सत्यम् वक्ष्यामि। — *makaro na vadati. sa cintayati — idānīṃ satyaṃ vakṣyāmi.* — "The crocodile
   does not speak. He thinks: 'Now I will tell the truth.'"
6. **किं मकरः सत्यं वक्ष्यति?** — pause: किम् मकरः सत्यम् वक्ष्यति? — *kiṃ makaraḥ
   satyaṃ vakṣyati?* — "Will the crocodile tell the truth?"

Questions: (a) Read रक्तमुखः as a bahuvrīhi: what does it say of the monkey? (his face is red)
(b) What does the monkey ask? (why the crocodile goes so fast). वक्ष्यति: ब्रवीति's future, printed on
the c03.l05 card. Glossed: 1 (रक्तम्).

### §18 Exercises

**Ex 1 (objective 1; 8 items).** Make the compound from its vigraha: (1) ग्रामस्य जनः (2) नृपस्य मन्त्री
(3) देवस्य पूजा (4) राज्ञः पुत्रः (5) राज्ञः गृहम् (6) मृगाणां राजा (7) वानराणां राजा (8) जलस्य तीरम्.
*Key:* (1) ग्रामजनः (2) नृपमन्त्री (3) देवपूजा (4) राजपुत्रः [8.2.7] (5) राजगृहम् [8.2.7; Monier-Williams s.v. rāja-gṛha, "a king's house, palace"] (6) मृगराजः [5.4.91] (7) वानरराजः [5.4.91]
(8) जलतीरम्. One point each.

**Ex 2 (objective 2; 6 items).** Make the compound "born in X" (-ज), nominative singular, and give its
vigraha with जायते: (1) समुद्रे (2) देशे (3) गृहे (4) नद्याम् (5) अग्नौ (6) भूम्याम्.
*Key:* (1) समुद्रजम् [समुद्रे जायते इति] (2) देशजः (3) गृहजः (4) नदीजः (5) अग्निजः (6) भूमिजः — the
masculine or the neuter accepted, the vigraha in each case "X-locative जायते इति". One point each.

**Ex 3 (objective 3; 6 items; derive-it-yourself).** (1) Derive, according to Pāṇini, जन्मनः दिनम् →
जन्मदिनम् in table form. (2) Derive, according to Pāṇini, पङ्के जायते इति → पङ्कजम्. (3) Derive, according to Pāṇini, महान् राजा → महाराजः.
(4)–(6) Answer according to Pāṇini. (4) In राजपुरुषः, why does the न् of राजन् drop? (5) In जलजम्, what drops from जन् before ड, and why? (6) In
कपोतराजः, what is the final -अ before the ending?
*Key:* (1) F-r (2) F-s (3) F-q (4) राजन् is a pada, and the न् at the end of a nominal base that is a
pada drops (8.2.7) (5) its ṭi, अन् (6.4.143 with 1.1.64): ड is ḍ-marked (6) the samāsānta टच्, after
-राजन् at a tatpuruṣa's end (5.4.91).

**Ex 4 (objective 4; 8 items).** (1)–(5) Give the vigraha and name the kind: (1) नीलकमलम् (2) सूर्यचन्द्रौ
(3) बहुधनः (4) विद्यालयः (5) मातापितरौ. (6) Name the kind of सुप्रभातम्. (7) Read इन्द्रशत्रुः as a
tatpuruṣa: its vigraha and meaning. (8) Read it as a bahuvrīhi: its vigraha and meaning.
*Key:* (1) नीलं कमलम् — karmadhāraya (2) सूर्यश्च चन्द्रश्च — dvandva (3) बहु धनं यस्य सः — bahuvrīhi
(4) विद्याया आलयः — tatpuruṣa (genitive) (5) माता च पिता च — dvandva (6) a prādi compound, by 2.2.18 (7)
इन्द्रस्य शत्रुः — "Indra's enemy" (8) इन्द्रः शत्रुः यस्य सः — "he whose enemy is Indra". Either analysis
of a compound with two readings is accepted where no context decides (the rule settled at v3.c05).

### §19 Quiz (correct option first)

1. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, the endings inside राजपुरुषः are — **deleted** · kept · moved to its
   end
2. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, राजन् loses its न् in राजपुरुषः because — **the first member is a
   pada, and the न् at the end of a nominal base that is a pada drops** · the न् is a marker · the
   compound is a bahuvrīhi
3. [FACT] "The king of the doves", one word: — **कपोतराजः** · राजपुरुषः · महाराजः
4. [FACT] जले जायते इति — **जलजम्** · खगः · जलम्
5. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, before ड, जन् loses — **its ṭi, अन्** · its first sound · nothing
6. [FACT] "A great king", one word: — **महाराजः** · राजपुरुषः · कपोतराजः
7. [FACT] नीलः कण्ठो यस्य सः — the kind: — **bahuvrīhi** · dvandva · tatpuruṣa
8. [FACT] इन्द्रशत्रुः read as a bahuvrīhi means — **he whose enemy is Indra** · Indra's enemy · Indra and
   his enemy
9. [FACT] वार्ता means — **news** · a road · birth
10. [FACT] The 1.x book glosses इन्द्रशत्रु as "Indra's slayer" and as "the slayer of Indra". — **The
    two glosses say the same thing; the contrast is tatpuruṣa against bahuvrīhi** · both are right as
    printed · the second reading is a dvandva

  (2) जन्मनः दिनम् (3) कपोतानां राजा (4) महान् राजा (5) सिंहश्च मूषकश्च (6) नीलः कण्ठो यस्य सः; v4.c04.l05's
  Station 0 runs exactly this.

  a bahuvrīhi, and say in English what each means; v4.c05.l01's Station 0 runs exactly this.

· 1.2.42 तत्पुरुषः समानाधिकरणः कर्मधारयः (the name) · 2.1.23 द्विगुश्च · 2.1.5 अव्ययीभावः · 2.2.6 नञ् and

गतिश्च, 1.4.61 ऊर्यादिच्विडाचश्च (ऊरीकृत्य, स्वीकृत्य) · 6.4.144 नस्तद्धिते (कपोतराजन् + अ; K59) · 1.1.62

**Payoff.** जलज (v1.c03.l02) is derived; the कपोतराजः pointer of v3.c05.l04 is redeemed; राजपुरुषः shows
the steps every compound takes; Volume 3's "What waits" (v3.c05.l04, lines 846–853) — accent, the
prādi, gati and upapada kinds, the -राज and -सख ends — is answered; the negative अ- and महा- labels of
v3.c05.l05's ledger are named.

**Previews and deferrals.** The n-stem cells (जन्मन्, मन्त्रिन्, राजन्): c05.l03. Accent beyond the one
card: Volume 5. Avyayībhāva in production: ◐.

**Verified pointers.** v3.c05.l04 lines 846–853 ("What waits") and 1270–1287 (the Teacher Notes on
सुप्रभातम् and on p. 82); v3.c05.l05 line 731 (the labels row); v1.c03.l02 (जलज "water-born; lotus", the
ledger); v4.c02.l01 (राज्ञा, 8.2.7); v4.c03.l03's anubandha card (the ड् row); v3.c06.l03 (रक्तमुखो नाम
वानरः).

**The 1.x page (X1).** Its "after 3,500+ years" is not repeated in learner text: no number with a
source and a method.

**Main risk:** "compounds have no grammar" — the six steps, said for every compound of the lesson.

## 5. L5 — चतुर्थपरीक्षा: the Chapter 4 Exam (review)

**Front matter.** id `v4.c04.l05` · file `lesson-05-chapter-four-exam.md` · title sa चतुर्थपरीक्षा · sa_iast
*caturthaparīkṣā* · en "Chapter 4 Exam" · hi "चौथे अध्याय की परीक्षा" · lesson_type review · cefr B1 ·
duration 45 · self_study_minutes **40** (15 before the sitting, 25 after) · prerequisites [v4.c04.l04]
· review_of [v4.c04.l01, v4.c04.l02, v4.c04.l03, v4.c04.l04, v4.c03.l06] · vocabulary_count **0** · tags
[review, exam, krt, participle, absolutive, infinitive, agent-noun, taddhita, samasa, derivation,
toolbox-card, hitopadesha, unseen-reading, spiral, spaced-review] · media (planned): image
`v4c04l05-chapter-on-one-page.svg` (the kṛt card, the taddhita table and the compound steps as three
panels); worksheet `v4c04l05-exam-booklet.pdf` (the Station 0 slip, the cover, the task pages and the
loose toolbox card); quizzes `v4c04l05-quiz.md`, `v4c04l05-quiz-key.md`.

**Objectives (one per task):**
1. en "Form the -त, the -तवत् and the present participle of ten owned verbs." · hi "दस परिचित क्रियाओं
   के -त, -तवत् और वर्तमान कृदन्त रूप बनाएँ।" (task A)
2. en "Join actions of one doer with absolutives, and form -तुम् and -तृ." · hi "एक कर्ता की क्रियाओं को
   पूर्वकालिक कृदन्त से जोड़ें, और -तुम् तथा -तृ रूप बनाएँ।" (task B)
3. en "Derive, according to Pāṇini, with the toolbox card open, one form from each of lessons 2–4." ·
   hi "सूत्र-कार्ड खुला रखकर, पाणिनि के अनुसार, पाठ 2–4 में से हर एक का एक रूप व्युत्पन्न करें।" (task C)
4. en "Read an unseen Hitopadeśa passage with nine glosses and say who did what, and what first." · hi
   "नौ टिप्पणियों के साथ हितोपदेश का एक अनदेखा अंश पढ़ें और बताएँ कि किसने क्या किया, और पहले क्या।"
   (task D)
5. en "Write a six-sentence account of a day with two absolutive chains, an infinitive of purpose and
   two -त forms with the doer in the instrumental." · hi "एक दिन का छह वाक्यों का वर्णन लिखें — दो
   पूर्वकालिक-शृंखलाएँ, एक प्रयोजन का तुमुन्, और कर्ता तृतीया में रखकर दो -त रूप।" (task E)

**Format.** One closed-book sitting of 45 minutes, 2 of them for instructions; Station 0 (about ten
minutes) runs before it and outside it, so the room time is about 55 minutes. The booklet prints
every task; a loose sheet prints the toolbox card of 131, handed out at minute 17, when task C
begins, and collected at minute 27, when it ends: task C is the one task with the card open.

### §3 Station 0 (about 10 minutes, before the sitting and outside it)

> **Station 0 — स्मरणम्**
> - +1 day: six compounds — make the compound of each phrase and name its kind: (1) राज्ञः पुरुषः
>   (2) जन्मनः दिनम् (3) कपोतानां राजा (4) महान् राजा (5) सिंहश्च मूषकश्च (6) नीलः कण्ठो यस्य सः;
>   v4.c04.l05's Station 0 runs exactly this. **(4 min)**
> - +1 week: derive पाणिनीयम् — derive, according to Pāṇini, in table form, पाणिनि + छ → पाणिनीयम्, each
>   step with its sūtra; v4.c04.l05's Station 0 runs exactly this. **(5 min: 4 to write, 1 to mark)**
> - Prompts (unscored, 1 min, said aloud): the two frames of -त, one sentence each (v4.c04.l01) · the
>   k-marker's three jobs, one form each (v4.c04.l02) · भवति's future, 3rd singular (v4.c03.l06)

Key (✓ only): S0-fg and S0-ff as table 0.I; prompts: मया पुस्तकं पठितम्; रामो वनं गतः · कृत्वा (no guṇa),
गत्वा (the nasal gone), उक्त्वा (samprasāraṇa) · भविष्यति. The teacher says aloud before the sitting
that task C will not ask पाणिनीयम् and that no task asks the six compounds of item 1 (the rule settled at

  — pause: वानरः प्रभाते वृक्षात् आगत्य नद्याम् स्नात्वा फलानि खादति। — "In the morning the monkey comes

| 2.2.18 | कुगतिप्रादयः | "कु, the gati words and प्र and the rest always compound with a word able to join them; the compound is a tatpuruṣa." | D · c04.l04 |
| 2.2.19 | उपपदमतिङ् | "An upapada that is not a tiṅ-final word always compounds with the word it goes with (the kṛt form): a tatpuruṣa." | D · c04.l04 |
| 2.4.71 | सुपो धातुप्रातिपदिकयोः | "A case ending inside a root or a nominal base is deleted." | D · c04.l04 |

| 3.2.97 | सप्तम्यां जनेर्डः | "After जन्, with a word in the locative as upapada, ड." | D · c04.l04 |
| 3.2.102 | निष्ठा | "The niṣṭhā comes after a root for past time." | D · c04.l01 |

| 3.4.21 | समानकर्तृकयोः पूर्वकाले | "[After a root,] when two actions have the same doer, [क्त्वा] for the earlier." | Q · c04.l02 |
| 4.1.4 | अजाद्यतष्टाप् | "After a base in अ, in the feminine, टाप्." | D · c04.l03 |
| 4.1.76 | तद्धिताः | "[From here to the end of Book 5, the affixes are called] taddhita." | D · c04.l03 |
| 4.1.92 | तस्यापत्यम् | "In the sense 'X's offspring'." | D · c04.l03 |
| 4.1.95 | अत इञ् | "After a base in अ, in the sense 'offspring', इञ्." | D · c04.l03 |
| 4.2.114 | वृद्धाच्छः | "After a vṛddha base, छ." | D · c04.l03 |
| 5.1.44 | लोकसर्वलोकाट्ठञ् | "After लोक and सर्वलोक, in the sense 'known in it', ठञ्." | D · c04.l03 |
| 5.1.119 | तस्य भावस्त्वतलौ | "In the sense 'the being of X', त्व and तल्." | D · c04.l03 |
| 5.2.94 | तदस्यास्त्यस्मिन्निति मतुप् | "In the sense 'X is his' or 'X is in it', मतुप्." | D · c04.l03 |
| 5.2.115 | अत इनिठनौ | "After a base in अ, in the same sense, इनि or ठन्." | D · c04.l03 |
| 5.4.91 | राजाहस्सखिभ्यष्टच् | "After a tatpuruṣa ending in राजन्, अहन् or सखि, the samāsānta टच्." | D · c04.l04 |
| 6.1.15 | वचिस्वपियजादीनां किति | "Before a k-marked affix, वच्, स्वप्, and यज् and the rest take samprasāraṇa." | D · c04.l01 |
| 6.1.71 | ह्रस्वस्य पिति कृति तुक् | "After a short vowel, before a p-marked kṛt, the augment तुक्." | D · c04.l02 |
| 6.3.46 | आन्महतः समानाधिकरणजातीययोः | "Before a second member in the same case, महत् → महा." | D · c04.l04 |
| 6.4.37 | अनुदात्तोपदेशवनतितनोत्यादीनामनुनासिक लोपो झलि क्ङिति | "Before a k- or ṅ-marked affix beginning with a jhal, the nasal of the roots taught unaccented (गम्, मन्, हन्, यम्, रम्, नम्), of वन् and of तन् and the rest drops." | D · c04.l01 |
| 6.4.38 | वा ल्यपि | "Before ल्यप् the nasal loss [of 6.4.37] is optional for a root in म्; for the others the loss is obligatory." | D · c04.l02 |
| 6.4.143 | टेः | "Before a ḍ-marked affix, the ṭi drops." | D · c04.l04 |
| 6.4.148 | यस्येति च | "Of a bha base ending in इ/ई or अ/आ, the final vowel is dropped before ई and before a taddhita." | D · c04.l03 |
| 7.1.2 | आयनेयीनीयियः फढखच्छघां प्रत्ययादीनाम् | "At an affix's beginning, फ, ढ, ख, छ, घ are replaced by आयन्, एय्, ईन्, ईय्, इय्." | D · c04.l03 |
| 7.1.37 | समासेऽनञ्पूर्वे क्त्वो ल्यप् | "In a compound whose first member is not नञ्, क्त्वा is replaced by ल्यप्." | D · c04.l02 |

| 8.2.9 | मादुपधायाश्च मतोर्वोऽयवादिभ्यः | "After a base ending in म् or अ/आ, or with म् or अ/आ before its last sound, the म् of मतुप् becomes व् — not after यव and the rest." | D · c04.l03 |
| 8.2.30 | चोः कुः | "A c-row sound becomes a k-row sound before a jhal and at the end of a pada." | D · c04.l01 |
| 8.3.24 | नश्चापदान्तस्य झलि | "न् or म् not at the end of a pada becomes anusvāra before a jhal." | D · c04.l02 |

### §16 Conversation (eight turns; read aloud before the sitting, §21 item 3)

A teacher (गुरुः) and a student (छात्रः) on the eve of the exam.

1. गुरुः — **श्वः परीक्षा। त्वं सर्वं पठितवान्?** — pause: श्वः परीक्षा। त्वम् सर्वम् पठितवान्? — *śvaḥ
   parīkṣā. tvaṃ sarvaṃ paṭhitavān?* — "Tomorrow is the exam. Have you studied everything?" (परीक्षा
   glossed "exam": the title's word.)
2. छात्रः — **आम्, गुरो। मया सर्वाणि सूत्राणि पठितानि।** — pause: आम्, गुरो। मया सर्वाणि सूत्राणि पठितानि। —
   *ām, guro. mayā sarvāṇi sūtrāṇi paṭhitāni.* — "Yes, teacher. All the sūtras have been read by me."
3. गुरुः — **गत्वेति रूपे मकारः कुत्र गतः?** — pause: गत्वा इति रूपे मकारः कुत्र गतः? — *gatveti rūpe makāraḥ
   kutra gataḥ?* — "In the form गत्वा, where has the म् gone?"
4. छात्रः — **क्त्वा कित्, अतो मकारो न भवति।** — pause: क्त्वा कित्, अतः मकारः न भवति। — *ktvā kit, ato
   makāro na bhavati.* — "क्त्वा is k-marked, so there is no म्."
5. गुरुः — **लौकिकमिति शब्दे कस्तद्धितः?** — pause: लौकिकम् इति शब्दे कः तद्धितः? — *laukikam iti śabde
   kas taddhitaḥ?* — "In the word लौकिकम्, which taddhita?"
6. छात्रः — **ठञ्, गुरो। अत आदौ वृद्धिः।** — pause: ठञ्, गुरो। अतः आदौ वृद्धिः। — *ṭhañ, guro. ata ādau
   vṛddhiḥ.* — "ठञ्, teacher. So there is vṛddhi at the beginning." (आदौ "at the beginning", glossed.)
7. गुरुः — **भद्रम्। श्वः प्रभाते शीघ्रमागच्छ।** — pause: भद्रम्। श्वः प्रभाते शीघ्रम् आगच्छ। — *bhadram.
   śvaḥ prabhāte śīghram āgaccha.* — "Good. Come early tomorrow morning."
8. छात्रः — **आगमिष्यामि, गुरो।** — pause: आगमिष्यामि, गुरो। — *āgamiṣyāmi, guro.* — "I will come,
   teacher."

Seams: गत्वेति (आ + इ → ए); लौकिकमिति (m + vowel joined); कस्तद्धितः (visarga → स् before त); अतो मकारः (अः → ओ
before a voiced sound); अत आदौ (अः before a vowel other than अ: the visarga
drops); ṃ; m + vowel joined. गुरो: the vocative of गुरुः (Volume 3's u-stem cells). सूत्राणि पठितानि:
the -त agrees with the neuter plural (l01's frame).

### §17 The spiral (after the sitting; unscored)

1. Derive, according to Pāṇini, नी + लट् → नयति, as v4.c03.l02 derived it (§13 derivation (a), worked in §8 Step 4: the
   table of seven steps); key: that table.
2. Re-read the thread verse: **उद्यमेन हि सिध्यन्ति कार्याणि न मनोरथैः। न हि सुप्तस्य सिंहस्य प्रविशन्ति मुखे
   मृगाः॥** — and say what Chapter 4 adds to it: सुप्तस्य is the -त of स्वप् (स्वपिति), with samprasāraṇa
   before the k-marked क्त, व् → उ (6.1.15, by gist), in the genitive: "of a sleeping lion"; मनोरथैः is a
   compound (मनस् + रथ: its s-stem first member c05.l03's). Key in §19's key file.

### §18 The tasks (booklet)

**कार्यम् A · participles (7 min; 30 marks; pass 22).** For each verb write the -त form, the -तवत् form
and the present participle, masculine nominative singular: (1) करोति (2) गच्छति (3) पठति (4) पश्यति
(5) तिष्ठति (6) जयति (7) त्यजति (8) शृणोति (9) नयति (10) मन्यते.
*Key:* (1) कृतः · कृतवान् · कुर्वन् [कुर्वाणः accepted] (2) गतः · गतवान् · गच्छन् (3) पठितः · पठितवान् · पठन्
(4) दृष्टः · दृष्टवान् · पश्यन् (5) स्थितः · स्थितवान् · तिष्ठन् (6) जितः · जितवान् · जयन् (7) त्यक्तः ·
त्यक्तवान् · त्यजन् (8) श्रुतः · श्रुतवान् · शृण्वन् (9) नीतः · नीतवान् · नयन् (10) मतः · मतवान् · मन्यमानः.
One mark per form. (No वच्, which has no present participle on the card; no -यमान passive form.)

**कार्यम् B · absolutive, infinitive, agent (8 min; 20 marks; pass 15).** B1 (12 marks): join each pair
with -त्वा, or -य after a prefix — two marks each, the form and the joined sentence: (1) छात्रः पुस्तकं
गृह्णाति। छात्रो विद्यालयं गच्छति। (2) माता वचनं शृणोति। माता वदति। (3) वानरो वृक्षमागच्छति। वानरः फलं
खादति। (4) पान्थो ग्रामं प्रविशति। पान्थो जलं पिबति। (5) गुरुः पत्रं ददाति। गुरुर्गच्छति। (6) सा मार्गमालोचयति।
सा वदति। B2 (4 marks): the -तुम् form: (1) स मित्रं ___ नगरं गच्छति। [दृश्] (2) बालिका कथां ___ इच्छति।
[श्रु] (3) अहं किमपि ___ न शक्नोमि। [कृ] (4) पान्थो ग्रामं ___ इच्छति। [गम्]. B3 (4 marks): the agent noun:
(1) one who goes (2) one who sees (3) one who enters — each nominative singular; (4) "the doers",
nominative plural.
*Key:* B1 (1) छात्रः पुस्तकं गृहीत्वा विद्यालयं गच्छति। (2) माता वचनं श्रुत्वा वदति। (3) वानरो वृक्षमागत्य फलं
खादति। [आगम्य accepted] (4) पान्थो ग्रामं प्रविश्य जलं पिबति। (5) गुरुः पत्रं दत्त्वा गच्छति। (6) सा
मार्गमालोच्य वदति। B2 (1) द्रष्टुम् (2) श्रोतुम् (3) कर्तुम् (4) गन्तुम् — before a consonant the joined द्रष्टुं, कर्तुं score the same. B3 (1) गन्ता (2) द्रष्टा (3) प्रवेष्टा
(4) कर्तारः.

**कार्यम् C · derivations (10 min; 18 marks; pass 13; the toolbox card open).** Derive, according to
Pāṇini, in table form, each step with its sūtra (number read off the card): (1) आ + गम् + क्त्वा → आगत्य
(2) लोक + ठञ् → लौकिकम् (3) जले जायते इति → जलजम्.
*Key:* F-f, F-j, F-o. Six marks each: (1) steps 0, 1, 3, 4, 5 and the last (the line of 2.2.18 is a
marked line: "आ + गम् make a compound, 2.2.18" scores within step 1); either route (on the आगम्य route, and for C′ (1)'s उपगम्य, step 4 is F-f's second-route line, "no तुक्: the root does not end in a
short vowel", 6.1.71 by number or gist); (2) steps 1, 3, 4, 5,
6, 7; (3) steps 1, 2 (the string; its rule is Teacher Notes), 3, 5, 6, 7. A step scores when its string
and its rule are both right; a labelled step scores for its string. The plan's "drawn at random from
the chapter set" (its main risk): C1 is the one item with a choice — the examiner may set गम् + क्त्वा →
गत्वा in its place (key F-g; six marks: steps 0, 1, 3, 4, and one each for "no इट्" and "the k-marker's
job in step 3"); C2 and C3 have no alternative, पाणिनीयम् and राजपुरुषः being Station 0 items of this
sitting.

**कार्यम् D · an unseen reading (11 min; 12 marks; pass 8).** N3b, as table 0.J prints it, joined, with
its nine glosses in the margin and the frame line: "The story of the old tiger and the traveller goes
on; the tiger is speaking." Six questions, two marks each (English answers accepted; a Sanskrit word from
the passage scores the same): (1) What does the tiger tell the traveller to do before he takes the
bracelet? (2) Why does the traveller go into the lake? (3) What happens as soon as he enters it?
(4) What does the tiger do first — see the traveller, or speak? (5) What does the tiger say he will
do? (6) After saying it, what does the tiger do, and what happens to the traveller?
*Key:* (1) bathe in the lake (सरसि स्नात्वा … गृहाण: first bathe, then take) (2) to bathe (स्नातुम्) — out
of greed, trusting the tiger's word (लोभात्; तद्वचःप्रतीतः): either half scores both marks (3) he sinks in
the deep mud and cannot get away (यावत् … तावत् महापङ्के निमग्नः पलायितुमक्षमः) (4) he sees him fallen in
the mud first (दृष्ट्वा), then speaks (अवदत्) (5) pull him out (उत्थापयामि) (6) he comes up slowly
(शनैः शनैरुपगम्य) and seizes him (धृतः); the traveller thinks (अचिन्तयत्). Teacher Notes, before the sitting
is marked: उक्त्वा and उपगम्य belong to the doer of धृतः, तेन व्याघ्रेण — the doer in the instrumental, l01's
frame — not to पान्थः, the subject of अचिन्तयत्; in a passive sentence "the gerund qualifies the agent in the
instrumental case" (Whitney §994c). No question asks गृहाण (◐).

**कार्यम् E · a day in six sentences (7 min; 14 marks; pass 10).** Write six sentences about your own
day, joined, with: two sentences each with two absolutives before the main verb (3 marks each: each
absolutive 1, the main verb 1); one sentence with -तुम् of purpose (2); two sentences with a -त form and
the doer in the instrumental (2 each: the form 1, the frame 1); one sentence of your own (1); then
write the pause form of one sentence (1). Only the card's verbs score. Model and key: §13 shows the
kind; the marker checks each target against §15 (1).

**Pass:** every task at its pass mark — A 22 of 30, B 15 of 20, C 13 of 18, D 8 of 12, E 10 of 14 — and the
§19 quiz, after the sitting, at 7 of 10. Total 94 marks.

### §19 Quiz (after the sitting; correct option first)

1. [FACT] मया पत्रं ___। — **लिखितम्** · लिखितः · लिखितवान्
2. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, the क् of क्त and क्त्वा is — **a marker: before such an affix guṇa
   does not apply** · part of the word · the sign of the future
3. [FACT] रामो वनं ___। "Rāma went to the forest." — **गतः** · गतम् · गन्तुम्
4. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, गन्तुम् keeps the nasal of गम्, written न् before त्, because — **तुमुन् has no क्** · गन्तुम् is a
   future · it has a prefix
5. [FACT] "Having bathed": — **स्नात्वा** · स्नातुम् · स्नातः
6. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, the first vowel of लौकिक took vṛddhi because — **ठञ् is ñ-marked** ·
   लोक is vṛddha · every taddhita gives vṛddhi
7. [FACT] धनमस्यास्तीति ___। — **धनवान्** · धनम् · धनस्य
8. [TRADITION] According to Pāṇini, राजन् becomes राज- in राजपुरुषः because — **it is a pada, and the न्
   at the end of a nominal base that is a pada drops** · the न् is a marker · राजन् is bha
9. [FACT] "Mud-born", a lotus: — **पङ्कजम्** · पङ्कः · जलम्
10. [FACT] In the reading of task D, what did the tiger do after he saw the traveller fallen in the
    mud? — **he spoke to him** · he bathed · he ran away

### §20 Revision — the bookings

- +1 day: two absolutives in one sentence — join each set of three into one sentence, making the first
  two verbs absolutives: (1) बालको विद्यालयं गच्छति। बालको मित्रं पश्यति। बालको हसति। (2) पान्थो ग्रामं
  प्रविशति। पान्थः कथां शृणोति। पान्थो गृहं गच्छति। (3) माता गृहमागच्छति। माता पुत्राय फलं ददाति। माता
  स्वपिति। v4.c05.l01's Station 0 runs exactly this.
- +1 week: participles — write the -त form, the -तवत् form and the present participle, masculine
  nominative singular, of पतति, कथयति, खादति, लिखति, जानाति and गृह्णाति; v4.c05.l02's Station 0 runs
  exactly this.

### §21 Homework (गृहकार्यम् — about 40 minutes: 15 before the sitting, 25 after)

Before the sitting: 1. (8, unscored) fill §15 (1)–(4) from memory on a sheet of your own, then check it
against §15. 2. (3) read once the toolbox card's rows for lessons 2–4 — their texts and English lines;
do not learn the numbers: the card is in your hands for task C. 3. (4) read §16 aloud, both roles, and
§13's three sentences, joined and then in their pause forms.
After the sitting: 4. (7, closed-book) the §19 quiz, then its key; 7/10 completes the exam. 5. (6) your
sitting against the task keys, task by task; for every lost mark, write the lesson and section the
key points to (§22). 6. (4, unscored) the §17 spiral and its key. 7. (5) one remediation exercise: your
lowest task's address in §22. 8. (3, real world) explain to a Hindi speaker, in Hindi, why "having gone"
is गत्वा and "to go" is गन्तुम्. Sum 8 + 3 + 4 + 7 + 6 + 4 + 5 + 3 = 40.

Tomorrow, before anything else: the +1-day item of §20. It runs as Station 0 of v4.c05.l01.

### §22 Teacher Notes

**Format and timing (the sitting, 45 min):** 0–2 instructions and hand-out → 2–9 A → 9–17 B → 17–27 C
(the toolbox card handed out at 17, collected at 27) → 27–38 D → 38–45 E. Sum 2 + 7 + 8 + 10 + 11 + 7 = 45.
Station 0 (§3: item 1 four, item 2 five, prompts one) runs before the sitting and outside it: book
about 55 minutes of room time. The minutes of A and D are each one fewer than the plan's shape (A 8,
D 12), to fit the two minutes of instructions inside the 45 (plan §7.6, the row on instructions; §7,
deviation 10).

**Remediation addresses (a task below its mark):** A — l01 §15 (1)–(2), then §18 Ex 1 and Ex 2; B — l02
§18 Ex 1 (B1), Ex 2 (B2), Ex 4 (B3); C — C1: l02 §18 Ex 3; C2: l03 §18 Ex 3; C3: l04 §18 Ex 3 (2); D — l01
§13 (the reading box) and §18 Ex 4, l02 §13 (the reading box); E — l02 §13 and §16, l01 §15 (5). The
retake of a task is taken one week later, with the set below; a retake item is never an item of its
task's remediation exercise, nor a derivation any lesson prints worked (§6 item 10).

**Retake sets (Teacher Notes only; they export as `examiner_only`).**

(9) रोचते (10) जायते. *Key:* भूतः भूतवान् भवन् · पीतः पीतवान् पिबन् · पृष्टः पृष्टवान् पृच्छन् · हृतः हृतवान्
हरन् · इष्टः इष्टवान् इच्छन् · प्रविष्टः प्रविष्टवान् प्रविशन् · चरितः चरितवान् चरन् · उदितः उदितवान् वदन् ·
रुचितः रुचितवान् रोचमानः · जातः जातवान् जायमानः. (None of these is in l01 Ex 1 or Ex 2.)

(3) नृपो नगरमागच्छति। नृपः सभां प्रविशति। (4) मकरो जलं प्रविशति। मकरः स्वपिति। (5) पिता पुत्राय धनं
ददाति। पिता गच्छति। (6) वानरो मकरं पश्यति। वानरो वृक्षं गच्छति। B′2 (4): (1) माता भोजनं ___ गृहं गच्छति।

[नी]. B′3 (4): (1) "the givers", nominative plural (2) त्वं ___ पश्यसि — "the doer", accusative singular
(3) "the goers", nominative plural (4) अहं ___ पश्यामि — "the one who sees", accusative singular.
*Key:* B′1 (1) बालिका मालां गृहीत्वा नृत्यति। (2) गुरुः श्लोकं श्रुत्वा हसति। (3) नृपो नगरमागत्य सभां
प्रविशति। [आगम्य accepted] (4) मकरो जलं प्रविश्य स्वपिति। (5) पिता पुत्राय धनं दत्त्वा गच्छति। (6) वानरो
मकरं दृष्ट्वा वृक्षं गच्छति। B′2 (1) पक्तुम् (2) जेतुम् (3) हर्तुम् (4) नेतुम् — before a consonant the joined पक्तुं, हर्तुं score the same. B′3 (1) दातारः (2) कर्तारम्
(3) गन्तारः (4) द्रष्टारम्. (None in l02 Ex 1, Ex 2 or Ex 4.)

*C′ (18; pass 13; the same card):* Derive, according to Pāṇini, in table form, each step with its sūtra (number
read off the card): (1) उप + गम् + क्त्वा → उपगम्य or उपगत्य (2) माला + छ → मालीयम् (3) वने
जायते इति → वनजः. *Key:* F-t, F-u, F-v; scored as C. (None printed worked in any lesson; none in l02, l03
or l04 Ex 3.)

*D′ (12; pass 8):* Hit 2, kathā 8, line 2050, as table 0.J prints it, with its four glosses and the frame
line: "A hare has come late to a lion and said that another lion held him on the way. The lion,
angry, says: 'Show me that wicked one.'" (the content of lines 2046–2048, in English only) Six questions:
(1) Whom did the hare take with him? (2) Why did the hare go? (3) What did the hare do first, come
there or speak? (4) What did the hare say? (5) Where did he show the reflection? (6) Whose reflection
was it? *Key:* (1) the lion (तम्; सिंहस्य) (2) to show him a deep well (गभीरकूपं दर्शयितुम्) (3) he came
there first (आगत्य), then spoke (उक्त्वा) (4) "let my lord see for himself" (स्वयमेव पश्यतु स्वामी) (5) in
the water of the well (कूपजले) (6) the lion's own (तस्य सिंहस्यैव). The frame line is the course's English
summary of lines 2046 (its second sentence, which Chapter 3's D′ did not print) and 2048; it quotes
none of their words.

*E′ (14; pass 10):* the same task about the traveller's day before he met the tiger (from N3a; the
learner's own sentences), with the same targets and marks.

## 6. Pass C checklist

Each item names what to re-check, against what, and by what means; each is to be closed by Pass C
before the lessons ship.

1. **Sūtras** — every number and text printed in the five lessons and the frame, its padaccheda case
   labels (3.4.21, 7.2.117) and every bracket line of table 0.H, against the data file's `s`, `pc`,
   `an`, `ad` fields, by script (`br.py`, `sl.py` as run for this fact base). ## 7. Open items for review

**Open items.**
1. **The forms policy (row 0.2).** Confirm that a ✓ form printed in a lesson's table may be scored as a
   form although a K rule makes its shape (the -मान of 7.2.82, लब्ध of 8.2.40 and 8.4.53, the active गतः of
   3.4.72, the -तर/-तम of 5.3.57 and 5.3.55), the K rule itself being never asked. The alternative
   reading of Chapter 3's decision ("not even through an exercise's scoring of its result") would forbid
   these forms in every scored item, against the plan's ✓ for them; it would also need 7.2.82 raised to D
   (one more tool) for the -मान participles to be producible at all.
2. **Hindi parallels.** None prints (row 0.17); candidates for a plain-text line if Pass C opens a
   Hindi dictionary page for each: जन्मदिन, लौकिक, पंकज, उपर्युक्त; not राष्ट्रीय or भारतीय [row 0.17].
3. **D′ from Hit 2, kathā 8.** Line 2050 is unseen, but Chapter 3's D′ read lines 2044 and 2046 of the
   same story. The plan's §7.6 row allows it ("A later lesson that sets Hit 2, kathā 8 as unseen knows
   …"); the editor may prefer a passage of another story — none measured within the gloss limit.
4. **स्वभावः in Apte.** Closed: Apte prints it among the compounds of sva (-bhāvaḥ "own state … natural constitution, innate or peculiar disposition, nature"); both dictionaries are cited.
5. **-तवत् of every -त form.** Printed for every verb of the card on Whitney §959's general statement;
   Pass C may check the attestation of the intransitive ones (हसितवान्, चरितवान्, रुचितवान्, वृद्धवान्).
6. **The exam card's size.** All 131 rows (as Chapter 3 printed all 89), or only the rows task C needs
   (the §7.6 minimum)? The fact base proposes all 131.
7. **C1's choice** (आगत्य or गत्वा) is the examiner's; C2 and C3 have none (पाणिनीयम् and राजपुरुषः are this
   sitting's Station 0 items). The plan's "drawn at random" is met only for C1.
8. **वार्ता / वार्त्ता.** The course prints the Hitopadeśa's spelling; both dictionaries print the
   doubled one; every key accepts both.
9. **M2 in learner text.** Only its last sentence, paraphrased in English on the accent card; the
   Sanskrit stays in Teacher Notes.

1. N3a printed with the e-text's word division: पान्थेन आलोचितम्, भाग्येन एतत् stay apart (plan §5.3
   joined them), per the printing rule settled at the Chapter 3 review (STYLE_GUIDE §3.1).
2. N3a: no dash added after अपश्यम् (the plan's); the e-text has none.
3. N3a: *bho bho* kept as the e-text and the DCS text both print it (the plan's §4.6 changed it to भो
   भोः); *panthāḥ* corrected to पान्थाः on the DCS text's *pānthāḥ*.
4. l02 model sentence 4 re-worded (पङ्कात्स निर्गन्तुं न शक्नोति): the plan's पङ्कान्निर्गन्तुं crosses the ◐ seam
   त् + न.
5. l03 model sentence 5: छात्राः for the plan's शिष्याः (तावत् + शि- is the ◐ seam त् + श).

17. l01's +1-day booking (S0-fa) asks the -त and the -तवत् of the eight verbs, where the plan asks the -त alone.
18. Task A asks one present participle of करोति, कुर्वन्, with कुर्वाणः accepted, where the plan asks both for an ubhayapada verb such as कृ.
19. In l04, Ex 3 (1) has the learner derive जन्मदिनम् and Ex 3 (4) asks why राजन् loses its न्; राजपुरुषः is derived with the teacher (F-n), where the plan's main-risk line has the learner derive both.
20. N3b's गृहाण is L, a ◐ form of गृह्णाति, where the plan's §5.3 table has T: the plan's §7.6 row of the v4.c03 review (Pass B) keeps गृहाण ◐.
