**Editor's decisions (2026-11-18, after the fact-base verification):**

The verification confirmed 96 findings (2 blockers, 27 major, 67 minor) and rejected 2; 207 fixes
were applied, 22 were found already made by another checker's fix, and the 46 that collided with an
earlier fix were compared and kept in one wording (13 merged by hand). The two blockers, both fixed
above: 3.3.10's English line had the purpose backwards — तुमुन् goes after the root that names the
purpose, when another action is done for its sake, and every restatement now says so; and a
course-written joined line kept a visarga before a voiced consonant (तावच्छात्राः लिखन्ति →
तावच्छात्रा लिखन्ति). Among the majors: vṛddhi also comes with a k-marked taddhita (7.2.118), so no
line says "only ñ or ṇ"; दैवम्'s affix is अञ्, by the vārttika देवाद्यञञौ, not अण्; राष्ट्रीय and भारतीय
are not this chapter's derivations (4.2.93 gives राष्ट्रिय; 4.2.124 gives वुञ् after a vṛddha country
name); the STYLE_GUIDE §8 rule numbers are corrected (kṛt products 18, taddhitas 19, compounds 10,
the denominative आकर्णयति 17); the Pañcatantra frame lines are numbered from `vispancu.htm` itself
(5261–5285), and the serial no longer has the crocodile go slowly, which N4 contradicts; the
Whitney line for "-त्वा takes the root in the shape -त takes" is §991's own sentence, not §968a's,
which is on the infinitive; the Mahābhāṣya's इन्द्रशत्रु passage is a verse the Mahābhāṣya quotes,
printed with every seam the e-text leaves open; Teacher-Notes sūtra numbers no longer print in
learner tables; the 1.x book is not said to gloss पाणिनीय "made by"; and जलज is no longer called the
course's oldest compound.

1. **The forms policy** (open item 1): row 0.2 stands. A form printed ✓ in a lesson's table is a
   fact of the language and may be produced and scored as a form whatever rule makes it in Pāṇini's
   derivation; the rule itself is never scored. The Chapter 3 rule ("a K rule is never assessed,
   not even through an exercise's scoring of its result") forbids an item that has the learner
   apply a K rule and scores what it yields; it does not forbid a form the learner takes from a
   table, and Chapter 3 scored द्रक्ष्यति and लप्स्यते on that reading. The row on it in the
   corrections table above is entered in the plan's §7.6.
2. **Hindi parallels** (item 2): none prints in Chapter 4, and no drafter adds one. The candidate
   list stays for a later chapter that opens a Hindi dictionary page for each; राष्ट्रीय and
   भारतीय are struck from it.
3. **D′** (item 3): Hit 2, kathā 8, line 2050 is the retake's passage. It is unseen: Chapter 3's
   D′ read lines 2044 and 2046 of the same story, and line 2050 is neither. Teacher Notes say that
   a learner who sat Chapter 3's retake has read the story as far as the hare's first answer to the
   lion.
4. **स्वभावः** (item 4): closed by the verification — Apte prints it among the compounds of स्व;
   both dictionaries are cited.
5. **-तवत्** (item 5): the -तवत् of every -त of the card stays ✓. 3.2.102 with 1.1.26 makes क्तवतु
   after any root; Whitney §959 forms it "from the past passive participle, of whatever formation",
   and §960 adds: "Although originally and properly made only from transitive verbs …, it is
   finally found also from intransitives". l01 prints §960's sentence as a FACT line beside the
   card, with its ellipsis, so that a learner meets हसितवान् knowing it is the later usage. A
   regular cell is not attested form by form, as no -मान cell is.
6. **The exam card** (item 6): all 131 rows print, as Chapter 3 printed all 89; a row whose
   carried word a task asks prints no bracket (the Chapter 2 rule).
7. **C1** (item 7): the examiner draws C1 by lot, आगत्य or गत्वा, before the sitting; C2 and C3
   have no alternative, and Teacher Notes say why (their alternatives are this sitting's Station 0
   items).
8. **वार्ता** (item 8): the lessons print the Hitopadeśa's वार्ता; every key accepts वार्त्ता too;
   Teacher Notes name the dictionaries' spelling.
9. **M2** (item 9): as proposed — the accent card paraphrases its last sentence in English; the
   Sanskrit stays in Teacher Notes.
10. **Quiz option order**, as in Chapter 3: the listings above keep the correct option first; the
    drafter orders the options of every quiz so that `python3 scripts/quizcheck.py <lesson>` prints
    RESULT: PASS, and each explanation names the other options by the letters they have in the
    lesson. The lesson is the binding copy of the order.
